抱着胳膊，在寒气里打着盹。
西边的山上是不是
有什么在动？在——又不在。
也许是哪个狼性的夜游者，察觉
天亮，正在回家。又在哪儿了！
这时，鼾声连成的泡泡世界
碎了，一阵晕眩的风卷过
世界昏厥的废墟和
崩解的梦，变成一声喊：
“起床！起床！印第安人来了！起床！”
冷血骤然催成疯狂的恐惧，
心怦怦狂跳，人还醉在
残睡里，跌跌撞撞爬起，望见山冈
梦的影子还在那里飘——
不——是骑马的人正吼叫着冲下坡！
马匹挣着拴绳，
人立嘶鸣。卡宾枪连闪连暗，
把晕眩的图景抹脏。战鼓轰响

===Original===

And shaken rawhide crackled through the din.

A horse that trailed a bounding picket pin

Made off in terror. Others broke and fled.

Then suddenly the silence of the dead

Had fallen, and the slope in front was bare

And morning had become a startled stare

Across the empty prairie, white with frost.
Five horses and a pair of pack mules lost!

That left five donkeys for the packs. Men poked

Sly banter at the mountless ones, invoked

The “infantry” to back them, while they threw

The saddles on and, boot to belly, drew

Groan-fetching cinches tight.
A scarlet streak

Was growing in the east. Amid the reek

Of cowchip fires that sizzled with the damp

The smell of coffee spread about the camp

A mood of peace. But ’twas a lying mood;

For suddenly the morning solitude

Was solitude no longer. “Look!” one cried.

The resurrection dawn, as prophesied,

Lacked nothing but the trump to be fulfilled!

They wriggled from the valley grass! They spilled

Across the sky rim! North and south and west

Increasing hundreds, men and ponies, pressed

Against the few.
’Twas certain death to flee.

The way left open down the Rickaree

To where the valley narrowed to a gap

Was plainly but the baiting of a trap.

Who rode that way would not be riding far.

“Keep cool now, men! Cross over to the bar!”

The colonel shouted. Down they went pell-mell,

Churning the creek. A heaven-filling yell

Assailed them. Was it triumph? Was it rage?

Some few wild minutes lengthened to an age

While fumbling fingers stripped the horses’ backs

And tied the horses. Crouched behind the packs

And saddles now, they fell with clawing hands

To digging out and heaping up the sands

Around their bodies. Shots began to fall﻿—

The first few spatters of a thunder squall﻿—

And still the Colonel strolled about the field,

Encouraging the men. A pack mule squealed

And floundered. “Down!” men shouted. “Take it cool,”

The Colonel answered; “we can eat a mule

When this day’s work is over. Wait the word,

Then see that every cartridge wings a bird.

Don’t shoot too fast.”
The dizzy prairie spun

With painted ponies, weaving on the run

A many colored noose. So dances Death,

Bedizened like a harlot, when the breath

Of Autumn flutes among the shedding boughs

And scarlets caper and the golds carouse

And bronzes trip it and the late green leaps.

And then, as when the howling winter heaps

===Chinese===

抖动的生牛皮在喧声里噼啪炸响。
一匹拖着蹦跳的拴马桩的马
吓得逃走了。别的也挣脱四散。
随后突然间，死者一般的寂静
落了下来，正面的坡上空了，
早晨变成一声惊愕的凝望，
望着结霜的空旷草原。
丢了五匹马和两头驮骡！

驮子只剩五头驴来驮。人们
拿没了坐骑的人打趣，
请“步兵”给他们撑腰，一边
甩鞍上马，靴子顶着马肚，
勒紧了惹出呻吟的肚带。
东天一道猩红
正在变宽。牛饼火
带着潮气咝咝作响的烟气里，
咖啡的香味在营地四周
铺开一层安宁。可那是撒谎的安宁；
因为突然间，清晨的孤寂
不再是孤寂。“看！”有人喊。
复活的黎明，正如预言的那样，
只差一声号角就要应验！
他们从谷草里蠕动出来！他们
漫过天边！北、南、西三面
越聚越多的人马，挤压着
这一小撮。逃是必死。
里克里河下游留着的那条道，
通到山谷收窄成隘口的地方，
明明白白是陷阱的饵。
往那边骑的人骑不了多远。
“稳住，弟兄们！到沙洲上去！”
上校喊。他们一拥而下，
把小溪搅浑。一声贯天的呐喊
劈头压来。是得胜？是狂怒？
慌乱的手指剥下马背的驮具、
拴好马匹，这疯狂的几分钟
拉长成一个世纪。伏在驮包
和马鞍后面，他们伸出爪子般的手
在身子四周又刨又堆沙子。
枪声开始落下——
雷雨最初溅落的几滴——
上校仍在阵地上溜达，
给人打气。一头驮骡尖叫着
栽倒。“趴下！”人们喊。“沉住气，”
上校答；“今天活干完，
咱们还有骡肉吃。等口令，
每颗子弹都得撩倒一只鸟。
别打太快。”
晕眩的草原随彩绘的小马
旋转，边跑边编织
一根多色的绞索。死神就这样
浓妆艳抹像个荡妇起舞，当秋风
在落叶的枝间吹笛，
猩红在跳、金黄在闹、
古铜起舞、迟绿蹦跳。
然后，像嚎叫的冬天把

===Original===

The strippings of the hickory and oak

And hurls them in a haze of blizzard smoke

Along an open draw, the warriors formed

To eastward down the Rickaree, and stormed

Against the isle, their solid front astride

The shallow water.
“Wait!” the Colonel cried;

“Keep cool now!”﻿—Would he never say the word?

They heard the falling horses shriek; they heard

The smack of smitten flesh, the whispering rush

Of arrows, bullets whipping through the brush

And flicked sand *phutting*; saw the rolling eyes

Of war-mad ponies, crooked battle cries

Lost in the uproar, faces in a blast

Of color, color, and the whirlwind last

Of all dear things forever.
“*Now!*”
The fear,

The fleet, sick dream of friendly things and dear

Dissolved in thunder; and between two breaths

Men sensed the sudden splendor that is Death’s,

The wild clairvoyant wonder. Shadows screamed

Before the kicking Spencers, split and streamed

About the island in a flame-rent shroud.

And momently, with hoofs that beat the cloud,

Winged with the mad momentum of the charge,

A war horse loomed unnaturally large

Above the burning ring of rifles there,

Lit, sprawling, in the midst and took the air

And vanished. And the storming hoofs roared by.

And suddenly the sun, a handbreadth high,

Was peering through the clinging battle-blur.
Along the stream, wherever bushes were

Or clumps of bluejoint, lurking rifles played

Upon the isle﻿—a point-blank enfilade,

Horse-slaughtering and terrible to stand;

And southward there along the rising land

And northward where the valley was a plain,

The horsemen galloped, and a pelting rain

Of arrows fell.
Now someone, lying near

Forsyth, was yelling in his neighbor’s ear

“They’ve finished Sandy!” For a giant whip,

It seemed, laid hot along the Colonel’s hip

A lash of torture, and his face went gray

And pinched. And voices boomed above the fray,

“Is Sandy dead?” So, rising on a knee

That anyone who feared for him might see,

He shouted: “Never mind﻿—it’s nothing bad!”

And noting how the wild face of a lad

Yearned up at him﻿—the youngest face of all,

With cheeks like Rambeau apples in the fall,

Eyes old as terror﻿—“Son, you’re doing well!”

He cried and smiled; and that one lived to tell

The glory of it in the after days.
Now presently the Colonel strove to raise

The tortured hip to ease it, when a stroke

As of a dull ax bit a shin that broke

===Chinese===

山核桃和橡树的落叶扫拢成堆，
裹在一阵暴风雪的烟里，
沿着一道敞开的谷地猛掷，战士们
在里克里河下游的东面列阵，
冲向小岛，坚实的正面
跨在浅水上。
“等等！”上校喊；
“稳住！”——他到底什么时候下口令？
他们听见倒下的马匹嘶喊；听见
皮肉挨击的脆响、箭的
嗖嗖掠过、子弹抽打灌木、
弹起的沙子*噗噗*作响；看见
战疯的小马翻卷的眼睛、
被喧嚣吞没的歪斜战吼、
颜色、颜色的一阵狂风里
那张脸，和一切亲爱之物的
最后的旋风，永远。
“*现在！*”
恐惧，
那些亲爱之友飞掠而病态的梦
在雷霆里消散；两次呼吸之间，
人们触到死神独有的那种
突降的壮丽、狂野的洞视的惊奇。
影子在连踢的斯宾塞枪前
尖叫，裂开，披着火撕开的
裹尸布绕着小岛奔流。
而不时地，蹄声擂着云，
带着冲锋的疯狂动能张翼，
一匹战马大得反常地耸现在
那圈燃烧的步枪阵上方，
照亮、张开四蹄，在半空
腾起，又消失。冲击的蹄声雷鸣而过。
突然，太阳升了一掌高，
正从黏着的战雾里探头。
沿着河流，凡有灌木
或蓝节草丛的地方，埋伏的步枪
都对着小岛倾泻——抵近的纵射，
专屠马匹、站着就可怕；
南面沿隆起的地、
北面谷地变成平原的地方，
骑手们纵马奔驰，一阵密集的
箭雨落下。

这时有人趴在
福赛斯近旁，对着邻人的耳朵喊：
“桑迪完了！”像一条巨鞭，
滚烫地抽在上校的胯上，
一记酷刑的鞭痕，他的脸
灰了、缩了。喊声在厮杀上炸开：
“桑迪死了吗？”于是他单膝撑起，
让所有为他担心的人看见，
喊道：“没事——不碍的！”
他看见一张少年的狂野的脸
仰望着他——全队最年少的一张，
腮像秋天的朗博苹果，
眼睛老得像恐怖——“孩子，你打得很好！”
他喊着，笑了；那孩子后来活下来，
在往后的岁月里讲述这光荣。
这会儿上校正想抬起
受刑的胯换换姿势，猛地一击
像钝斧咬进他的一条腿骨，
在他的体重下折断了。

===Original===

Beneath his weight. Dragged backward in a pit,

He sat awhile against the wall of it

And strove to check the whirling of the land.

Then, noticing how some of the command

Pumped lead too fast and threw their shells away,

He set about to crawl to where they lay

And tell them. Something whisked away his hat,

And for a green-sick minute after that

The sky rained stars. Then vast ear-hollows rang

With brazen noises, and a sullen pang

Was like a fire that smouldered in his skull.

He gazed about him groggily. A lull

Had fallen on the battle, and he saw

How pairs of horsemen galloped down the draw,

Recovering the wounded and the dead.

The snipers on the river banks had fled

To safer berths; but mounted hundreds still

Swarmed yonder on the flat and on the hill,

And long range arrows fell among the men.
The island had become a slaughter pen.

Of all the mules and horses, one alone

Still stood. He wobbled with a gurgling moan,

Legs wide, his drooping muzzle dripping blood;

And some still wallowed in a scarlet mud

And strove to rise, with threshing feet aloft.

But most lay still, as when the spring is soft

And work-teams share the idleness of cows

On Sunday, and a glutted horse may drowse,

Loose-necked, forgetting how the plowshare drags.

Bill Wilson yonder lay like bundled rags,

And so did Chalmers. Farley over there,

With one arm limp, was taking special care

To make the other do; it did, no doubt.

And Morton yonder with an eye shot out

Was firing slowly, but his gun barrel shook.

And Mooers, the surgeon, with a sightless look

Of mingled expectation and surprise,

Had got a bullet just above the eyes;

But Death was busy and neglected him.
Now all the while, beneath the low hill rim

To southward, where a sunning slope arose

To look upon the slaughter, Roman Nose

Was sitting, naked of his battle-gear.

In vain his chestnut stallion, tethered near,

Had sniffed the battle, whinnying to go

Where horses cried to horses there below,

And men to men. By now a puzzled word

Ran round the field, and baffled warriors heard,

And out of bloody mouths the dying spat

The question: “Where is Roman Nose, the Bat?

While other men are dying, where is he?”

So certain of the mighty rode to see,

And found him yonder sitting in the sun.

They squatted round him silently. And one

Got courage for a voice at length, and said:

===Chinese===

他被拖回一个沙坑里，
靠着坑壁坐了一会儿，
竭力止住大地的旋转。
随后，注意到队里有些人
出铅太快、把弹壳都扔光了，
他着手朝他们趴的地方爬，
去告诫他们。什么东西嗖地掠掉
他的帽子，随后有一阵子
发绿恶心，天上落着星星。
接着巨大的耳窍里响起
黄铜的噪音，一股沉闷的痛
像一堆火在头颅里焖烧。
他晕乎乎地四下张望。战斗
落进了一个间歇，他看见
成对的骑手驰下谷地，
抢回伤者和死者。
河岸上的狙击手已经
逃去更稳当的位置；可几百骑
仍在那边的平地和山上蜂拥，
远程的箭落在人群里。
小岛已经变成一座屠宰栏。
所有的骡马里，只剩一头
还站着。它咕噜呻吟着打晃，
四腿岔开，垂下的口鼻滴着血；
还有几头在猩红的泥里
翻滚，踢蹬着四蹄想站起来。
但大多数都静静躺着，像春天
暖软时耕马同牛一起
过星期天，吃饱的马可以
垂着脖子打盹，忘了犁铧的拖坠。
比尔·威尔逊那边躺成一捆破布，
查默斯也是。那边的法利，
一条胳膊垂了，特别费心
叫另一条顶事；它确实顶住了，没错。
莫顿那边一只眼被打穿了，
还在慢慢地放枪，只是枪管在抖。
军医穆尔斯，带着
期待和惊讶掺半的失明的目光，
眉心上挨了一颗子弹；
可死神太忙，把他漏掉了。

而这一向，在南边低低的
山肩下，一面晒着太阳的坡
升起来观看着这场屠宰，
罗马鼻正坐在那里，卸去了战装。
他拴在近旁的栗色公马
嗅着战斗，嘶鸣着要去
下面马唤马、人唤人的地方，
白费劲。这时一句疑惑的话
传遍战场，困惑的战士们听着，
垂死者从血嘴里吐出
那个问题：“罗马鼻那蝙蝠在哪儿？
别人都在死，他在哪儿？”
于是几位豪杰骑马去看，
发现他坐在那边的太阳里。
他们默默围着他蹲下。终于
有一个鼓起勇气开了口：

===Original===

“Your people there are dying, and the dead

Are many.” But the Harrier of Men

Kept silence. And the bold one, speaking then

To those about him, said: “You see today

The one whom all the warriors would obey,

Whatever he might wish. His heart is faint.

He has not even found the strength to paint

His face, you see!” The Flame of Many Roofs

Still smouldered there. The Midnight Wind of Hoofs

Kept mute. “Our brothers, the Arapahoes,”

Another said, “will tell of Roman Nose;

Their squaws will scorn him; and the Sioux will say

‘He was not like the men we were that day

When all the soldiers died by Peno ford!’ ”
They saw him wince, as though the words had gored

His vitals. Then he spoke. His voice was low.

“My medicine is broken. Long ago

One made a bonnet for a mighty man,

My father’s father; and the good gift ran

From sire to son, and we were men of might.

For he who wore the bonnet in a fight

Could look on Death, and Death would fear him much,

So long as he should let no metal touch

The food he ate. But I have been a fool.

A woman lifted with an iron tool

The bread I ate this morning. What you say

Is good to hear.”
He cast his robe away,

Got up and took the bonnet from its case

And donned it; put the death-paint on his face

And mounted, saying “Now I go to die!”

Thereat he lifted up a bull-lunged cry

That clamored far among the hills around;

And dying men took courage at the sound

And muttered “He is coming.”
Now it fell

That those upon the island heard a yell

And looked about to see from whence it grew.

They saw a war-horse hurtled from the blue,

A big-boned chestnut, clean and long of limb,

That did not dwarf the warrior striding him,

So big the man was. Naked as the day

The neighbors sought his mother’s lodge to say

“This child shall be a trouble to his foes”

(Save for a gorgeous bonnet), Roman Nose

Came singing on the run. And as he came

Mad hundreds hailed him, booming like a flame

That rages over slough grass, pony tall.

They formed behind him in a solid wall

And halted at a lifting of his hand.

The troopers heard him bellow some command.

They saw him wheel and wave his rifle high;

And distant hills were peopled with the cry

He flung at Death, that mighty men of old,

Long dead, might hear the coming of the bold

And know the land still nursed the ancient breed.

Then, followed by a thundering stampede,

===Chinese===

“你的族人正在那边死去，死者
已经很多。”可这位人类的猎鹰
不作声。那个大胆的又转向
周围的人说：“你们今天看见了，
那个所有战士都愿听命的人，
无论他想要什么。他的心怯了。
他连往脸上涂战画的力气
都没找着，你们看！”万家灯火之焰
还在那里焖着。午夜的蹄风
沉默着。“我们的兄弟阿拉帕霍人，”
另一个说，“会讲起罗马鼻；
他们的女人会鄙视他；苏族会说
‘他不像佩诺渡口全军覆没那天的
我们这些人！’”
他们看见他一缩，仿佛那些话
抵进了他的脏腑。随后他开口了。
声音很低。“我的巫术破了。很久以前，
有人为一伟人做了一顶战冠，
我祖父的祖父；这份善礼
父传子、子传孙，我们一族
都是豪强。因为戴着这战冠作战的人
可以直视死神，死神也要怕他，
只要他不许任何金属碰
他吃的食物。可我做了个蠢人。
今天早上，一个女人用铁器
翻了我吃的饼。你们说的话，
正好听。”
他甩掉袍子，
起身从匣子里取出战冠
戴上；往脸上涂了死妆，
上马，说道：“我这就去死！”
随即他发出一声牛肺般的呐喊，
在四周的群山间传响；
垂死的人闻声壮了起来，
喃喃说“他来了。”
恰在这时
岛上的人听见一声呐喊，
四下张望它从何处长起。
他们看见一匹战马从蓝天里掷出，
一匹大骨的栗色马，四肢修长干净，
同马上挺进的战士正相称，
那人块头就有那么大。像邻居们
到他母亲的帐里报说
“这孩子日后必是他仇家的祸患”的
那一天一样赤裸（只多一顶华丽的战冠），
罗马鼻一路唱着奔来。
他一路奔来，疯狂的几百人
向他欢呼，轰响如燎过洼草的烈焰，
高可没马。他们在他身后
结成一堵实墙，他一抬手便停住。
士兵们听见他吼出一道口令。
他们看见他拨转马头，把步枪高举；
遥远的群山住满了他掷向
死神的呐喊，让久已死去的
古之豪杰听见勇者的到来，
知道这片土地仍哺育着古老的族类。
随后，身后跟着雷鸣的奔流，

===Original===

He charged the island where the rifles brawled.

And some who galloped nearest him recalled

In after days, what some may choose to doubt,

How suddenly the hubbuboo went out

In silence, and a wild white brilliance broke

About him, and the cloud of battle smoke

Was thronged with faces not of living men.

Then terribly the battle roared again.

And those who tell it saw him reel and sag

Against the stallion, like an empty bag,

Then slip beneath the mill of pony hoofs.
So Roman Nose, the Flame of Many Roofs,

Flared out. And round the island swept the foe﻿—

Wrath-howling breakers with an undertow

Of pain that wailed and murmuring dismay.
Now Beecher, with the limp he got that day

At Gettysburg, rose feebly from his place,

Unearthly moon-dawn breaking on his face,

And staggered over to the Colonel’s pit.

Half crawling and half falling into it,

“I think I have a fatal wound,” he said;

And from his mouth the hard words bubbled red

In witness of the sort of hurt he had.

“No, Beecher, no! It cannot be so bad!”

The other begged, though certain of the end;

For even then the features of the friend

Were getting queer. “Yes, Sandy, yes﻿—goodnight,”

The stricken muttered. Whereupon the fight

No longer roared for him; but one who grieved

And fought thereby could hear the rent chest heaved

With struggling breath that couldn’t leave the man.

And by and by the whirling host began

To scatter, most withdrawing out of range.

Astonished at the suddenness of change

From dawn to noon, the troopers saw the sun.
To eastward yonder women had begun

To glean the fallen, wailing as they piled

The broken loves of mother, maid and child

On pony-drags; remembering their wont

Of heaping thus the harvest of the hunt

To fill the kettles these had sat around.
Forsyth now strove to view the battleground,

But could not for the tortured hip and limb;

And so they passed a blanket under him

And four men heaved the corners; then he saw.

“Well, Grover, have they other cards to draw,

Or have they played the pack?” he asked a scout.

And that one took a plug of chewing out

And gnawed awhile, then spat and said: “Dunno;

I’ve fit with Injuns thirty year or so

And never see the like of this till now.

We made a lot of good ones anyhow,

Whatever else﻿—.”
Just then it came to pass

Some rifles, hidden yonder in the grass,

Took up the sentence with a snarling rip

===Chinese===

他冲向枪声喧的小岛。
有些贴着他驰骋的人后来
回忆——信不信随你——
喧嚣怎样突然熄灭
成寂静，一团狂野的白光
在他四周炸开，硝烟的云里
挤满了不是活人的脸。
随后战斗又可怕地轰响起来。
讲述这事的人看见他一晃，
像一只空口袋软倒在公马颈上，
随即滑进小马蹄的磨盘下面。
罗马鼻，万家灯火之焰，
就这样烧尽了。敌潮绕岛扫过——
怒嚎的浪头，底下拖着一股
哀哭和低语着溃乱的痛。
这时比彻，拖着葛底斯堡
那天留下的瘸腿，从他的位置
虚弱地站起，一副非人间的
月光破晓照上他的脸，
踉跄着走向上校的沙坑。
半爬半跌地进到坑里，
“我怕是伤得致命，”他说；
嘴里吐出的硬字带着血泡，
证明他伤在哪一处。
“不，比彻，不！不会那么糟！”
另一个恳求着，其实已知道结局；
因为那时朋友的脸
已经在变形。“是的，桑迪，是的——晚安，”
伤者喃喃。对他来说，战斗
从此不再轰响；可一个
边打边悲恸的人听得见那破了的胸膛
怎样喘着挣不脱此身的呼吸。
渐渐地，旋转的敌群开始
散去，大多退出了射程。

惊讶于从黎明到正午的骤变，
士兵们看见了太阳。东边，
妇女们已开始拾捡阵亡者，
边哭边把母亲、少女和孩子
残破的珍爱堆上马拖，
想起往常这样堆起
猎获的收获去装满
这些人曾围坐的锅。福赛斯
想看一看战场，
可那受刑的胯和腿不许；
于是他们在他身下穿过一条毯子，
四人扯起四角抬着；他看见了。
“怎么样，格罗弗，他们还有牌可出吗，
还是一把全打光了？”他问一个侦察兵。
那人掏出一块嚼烟，
啃了一会儿，吐了口唾沫：“说不准；
我跟印第安人打了三十年上下，
从没见过这阵仗。不管怎么说，
咱们放倒了不少好手——。”
正在这时，藏在
那边草丛里的几支步枪
用一串咆哮接下了这半句

===Original===

That made men duck. One let his corner slip.

The Colonel tumbled, and the splintered shin

Went crooked, and the bone broke through the skin;

But what he said his angel didn’t write.
’Twas plain the foe had wearied of the fight,

Though scores of wary warriors kept the field

And circled, watching for a head revealed

Above the slaughtered horses. Afternoon

Waned slowly, and a wind began to croon﻿—

Like memory. The sapling cottonwood

Responded with a voice of widowhood.

The melancholy heavens wove a pall.

Night hid the valley. Rain began to fall.
How good is rain when from a sunlit scarp

Of heaven falls a silver titan’s harp

For winds to play on, and the new green swirls

Beneath the dancing feet of April girls,

And thunder-claps applaud the meadow lark!

How dear to be remembered﻿—rainy dark

When Youth and Wonder snuggle safe abed

And hear creation bustling overhead

With fitful hushes when the eave *drip-drops*

And everything about the whole house stops

To hear what now the buds and grass may think!
Night swept the island with a brush of ink.

They heard the endless drizzle sigh and pass

And whisper to the bushes and the grass,

*Sh﻿—sh*﻿—for men were dying in the rain;

And there was that low singing that is pain,

And curses muttered lest a stout heart break.
As one who lies with fever half awake

And sets the vague real shepherding a drove

Of errant dreams, the broken Colonel strove

For order in the nightmare. Willing hands

With knife and plate fell digging in the sands

And throwing out a deep surrounding trench.

Graves, yawning briefly in the inky drench,

Were satisfied with something no one saw.

Carved horse meat passed around for wolfing raw

And much was cached to save it from the sun.
Now when the work about the camp was done

And all the wounds had got rude handed care,

The Colonel called the men about him there

And spoke of Wallace eighty miles away.

Who started yonder might not see the day;

Yet two must dare that peril with the tale

Of urgent need; and if the two should fail,

God help the rest!
It seemed that everyone

Who had an arm left fit to raise a gun

And legs for swinging leather begged to go.

But all agreed with old Pierre Trudeau,

The grizzled trapper, when he “ ’lowed he knowed

The prairie like a farmer did a road,

And many was the Injun he had fooled.”

And Stillwell’s youth and daring overruled

===Chinese===

打得人们一缩。有个抬角的手一滑，
上校摔了下去，碎裂的胫骨
错了位，骨头穿出了皮；
但他骂了些什么，他的守护天使没有记下。
敌人显然打乏了，
虽然几十个警惕的战士还守着阵地，
兜着圈子，专等一颗脑袋
从死马堆后露出来。下午
慢慢偏西，风开始低唱——
像记忆。那株三角叶杨苗
应和着孀居的声音。
忧郁的天空织起一袭尸衣。
夜藏起了河谷。雨开始落。

雨多好啊，当一张银的巨人竖琴
从天国向阳的峭壁上垂下，
任风弹奏，新绿在
四月姑娘的舞足下旋起，
雷声为草地百灵鼓掌！
多么可念——落雨的黑夜，
青春和惊奇偎在安全的床上，
听造物在头顶上忙乱，
间歇地静下来，檐口*滴-滴答*，
整座房子里的一切都停下，
听花苞和草此刻在想什么！

夜用一支墨笔扫过小岛。
他们听见不停的细雨叹息着过去，
对灌木和草低语，
*嘘——嘘*——因为有人正在雨里死去；
有那种属于疼痛的低低的哼唱，
有压着嗓子的咒骂，怕一颗硬心碎掉。
像发着烧半醒躺着的人
让模糊的真实去收拢
一群走散的梦，碎了骨的上校
在噩梦里挣扎着求一个秩序。
甘愿的手拿着刀和铁盘
扑进沙里，挖出一圈深壕。
坟在墨黑的雨里张了张口，
被某种没人看见的东西满足了。
切下的马肉传开来生吞，
许多被藏起来躲避太阳。

营地上的活计做完、
所有的伤口都受到粗糙的照料后，
上校把人们叫到身边，
讲起八十英里外的华莱士。
往那边动身的人未必见得到天亮；
可必须有人去冒这个险，
带上急需救援的信；
倘若这两人失手，其余的人就听天由命！
似乎凡是还有一条
举得动枪的胳膊、一双
跨得上马镫的腿的人都求着要去。
但众人都赞成老皮埃尔·特鲁多，
那位灰胡子的设阱人，他“‘寻思自己认得
草原就像庄稼人认得路，
他糊弄过的印第安人海了去了’”。
于是斯蒂尔韦尔的年少和大胆压倒了

===Original===

The others. Big he was and fleet of limb

And for his laughing pluck men honored him,

Despite that weedy age when boys begin

To get a little conscious of the chin

And jokers dub them “Whiskers” for the lack.

These two were swallowed in the soppy black

And wearily the sodden night dragged by.
At last the chill rain ceased. A dirty sky

Leaked morning. Culver, Farley, Day and Smith

Had found a comrade to adventure with

And come upon the country that is kind.

But Mooers was slow in making up his mind

To venture, though with any breath he might.

Stark to the drab indecency of light,

The tumbled heaps, that once were horses, lay

With naked ribs and haunches lopped away﻿—

Good friends at need with all their fleetness gone.

Like wolves that smell a feast the foe came on,

A skulking pack. They met a gust of lead

That flung them with their wounded and their dead

Back to the spying summits of the hills,

Content to let the enemy that kills

Without a wound complete the task begun.
Dawn cleared the sky, and all day long the sun

Shone hotly through a lens of amethyst﻿—

Like some incorrigible optimist

Who overworks the sympathetic role.

All day the troopers sweltered in the bowl

Of soppy sand, and wondered if the two

Were dead by now; or had they gotten through?

And if they hadn’t﻿—What about the meat?

Another day or two of steaming heat

Would fix it for the buzzards and the crows;

And there’d be choicer banqueting for those

If no one came.
 So when a western hill

Burned red and blackened, and the stars came chill,

Two others started crawling down the flat

For Wallace; and for long hours after that

Men listened, listened, listened for a cry,

But heard no sound. And just before the sky

Began to pale, the two stole back unhurt.

The dark was full of shadow men, alert

To block the way wherever one might go.
Alas, what chance for Stillwell and Trudeau?

That day the dozen wounded bore their plight

Less cheerfully than when the rainy night

Had held so great a promise. All day long,

As one who hums a half forgotten song

By poignant bits, the dying surgeon moaned;

But when the west was getting sober-toned,

He choked a little and forgot the tune.

And men were silent, wondering how soon

They’d be like that.
Now when the tipping Wain,

Above the Star, poured slumber on the plain,

Jack Donovan and Pliley disappeared

===Chinese===

其余的人。他块头大、腿脚快，
那份笑着硬闯的胆气叫人敬他，
虽然正处在男孩开始
为下巴挂心的抽条年纪，
爱开玩笑的缺嘴叫他“胡子佬”。
这两个人被湿漉漉的黑夜吞没，
浸透的长夜挨了过去。
冷雨终于停了。脏兮兮的天空
漏下早晨。卡尔弗、法利、戴和史密斯
已找到一个同去冒险的伙伴，
到了那片仁慈的国土。
可穆尔斯还迟迟拿不定
决心动身，虽然他随时可能咽气。
灰白的光毫不体面地裸呈着：
那些曾经是马的一堆堆东西
赤着肋、卸掉了后臀躺着——
急难时的好朋友们，飞驰都没了。
像嗅到盛宴的狼群，敌人潜行而来，
一股偷偷摸摸的狼群。他们撞上一阵铅风，
连伤带死被刮回
窥伺的山顶，索性让这个
不带伤就能杀人的敌手
来完成已经开始的活计。

黎明晴了天，一整天太阳
透过一只紫水晶的透镜灼照——
像某个无可救药的乐天派，
把同情的戏演过了头。
士兵们在湿沙的碗里
整天焖着，想着那两个人
这会儿是不是死了；还是闯过去了？
要是没闯过去——肉怎么办？
再来一两天蒸人的热，
就便宜秃鹫和乌鸦了；
要是没人来，那些家伙
还有更丰盛的宴席。

于是在一面西山
烧红又转黑、星星带着寒意出来时，
又有两人爬下平川
奔华莱士去；此后好几个钟头，
人们听着、听着、听着一声喊，
什么也没听见。就在天
开始泛白之前，那两人悄悄爬了回来，
没有伤。黑暗里满是影子人，警醒着
堵住任何一条可能的路。
唉，斯蒂尔韦尔和特鲁多还有什么指望？

那天，十几个伤者对厄运的承受
不如那个落雨的夜
许下大希望时来得起劲。一整天，
垂死的军医像有人哼着
半忘的歌、只记得断片那样呻吟；
可当西天转成沉静的色调，
他哽了一下，忘了那支曲子。
人们沉默着，掂量自己
还有多久也会那样。
当倾侧的大车座
在明星之上把睡眠倾给平原，
杰克·多诺万和普利利不见了

===Original===

Down river where the starry haze made weird

The narrow gulch. They seemed as good as dead;

And all next day the parting words they said,

“We won’t be coming back,” were taken wrong.

The fourth sun since the battle lingered long.

Putrescent horseflesh now befouled the air.

Some tried to think they liked the prickly pear.

Some tightened up their belts a hole or so.

And certain of the wounded babbled low

Of places other than the noisome pits,

Because the fever sped their straying wits

Like homing bumblebees that know the hive.

That day the Colonel found his leg alive

With life that wasn’t his.
The fifth sun crept;

The evening dawdled; morning overslept.

It seemed the dark would never go away;

The kiotes filled it with a roundelay

Of toothsome horses smelling to the sky.
But somehow morning happened by and by.

All day the Colonel scanned the prairie rims

And found it hard to keep away the whims

That dogged him; often, wide awake, he dreamed.

The more he thought of it, the more it seemed

That all should die of hunger wasn’t fair;

And so he called the sound men round him there

And spoke of Wallace and the chance they stood

To make their way to safety, if they would.

As for himself and other cripples﻿—well,

They’d take a chance, and if the worst befell,

Were soldiers.
There was silence for a space

While each man slyly sought his neighbor’s face

To see what better thing a hope might kill.

Then there was one who growled: “The hell we will!

We’ve fought together and we’ll die so too!”

One might have thought relief had come in view

To hear the shout that rose.
The slow sun sank.

The empty prairie gloomed. The horses stank.

The kiotes sang. The starry dark was cold.
That night the prowling wolves grew over bold

And one was cooking when the sun came up.

It gave the sick a little broth to sup;

And for the rest, they joked and made it do.

And all day long the cruising buzzards flew

Above the island, eager to descend;

While, raucously prophetic of the end,

The crows wheeled round it hungrily to pry;

And mounted warriors loomed against the sky

To peer and vanish. Darkness fell at last;

But when the daylight came and when it passed

The Colonel scarcely knew, for things got mixed;

The moment was forever, strangely fixed,

And never in a moment. Still he kept

One certain purpose, even when he slept,

===Chinese===

顺河而下，星雾把窄窄的
峡谷弄得怪异。他们跟死了差不多；
第二天一整天，他们临别的那句
“我们回不来了”，被大家听拧了。
开战以来第四个太阳迟迟不去。
腐烂的马肉玷污了空气。
有人努力觉得自己喜欢仙人掌果。
有人把腰带收紧了一两个孔。
有几个伤者低声念叨着
恶臭的沙坑以外的地方，
因为热病催着他们走神的脑子，
像认得蜂巢的回家的大黄蜂。
那天上校发现他的腿活着，
用一种不属于他的生命。第五个太阳
爬行；黄昏磨蹭；早晨睡过了头。
黑暗仿佛永远不会散；
郊狼拿一支好吃的马肉的
轮唱歌把它填满。可早晨不知怎么
还是慢慢来了。

上校整天瞭望草原的边缘，
很难撵开那些缠人的怪念头；
常常睁着眼就做起梦来。
他越想越觉得
全队人都饿死不公平；
于是他把还能走的叫到身边，
讲起华莱士，讲他们若肯走，
还有一条逃生的机会。
至于他自己和别的残废——嗯，
他们可以赌一把，万一落到最坏，
到底是当兵的。
一时没有人作声，
每个人斜着眼找邻人的脸，
看希望会杀掉哪样更好的东西。
随后有人吼道：“见鬼的才走！
咱们一起打过仗，死也要一起死！”
听见那一声喊，谁都会以为
救兵已经望得见了。

迟缓的太阳落了。
空旷的草原暗了。马尸发臭。
郊狼唱了。星空的黑夜很冷。
那夜，游荡的狼胆子过了头，
太阳出来时，有一只正在锅里炖着。
它给病号们匀了点汤喝；
其余的人拿它打趣，将就着吃。
盘旋的秃鹫整日在
小岛上空飞，急着落下来；
乌鸦吵哑地预言着结局，
饿着肚子绕岛盘旋窥探；
骑马的战士衬着天空出现，
张望一下又隐没。黑夜终于降临；
可白天什么时候来、什么时候去，
上校已经分不清，事情都混了；
那一刻成了永远，怪怪地定住，
又从不停在哪一刻里。但他仍抱着
一个确定的念头，连睡着时也是：

===Original===

To cheer the men by seeming undismayed.

But when the eighth dawn came, he grew afraid

Of his own weakness. Stubbornly he sat,

His tortured face half hidden by his hat,

And feigned to read a novel one had found

Among the baggage. But the print went round

And wouldn’t talk however it was turned.
At last the morning of the ninth day burned.

Again he strove to regiment the herds

Of dancing letters into marching words,

When suddenly the whole command went mad.

They yelled; they danced the way the letters had;

They tossed their hats.
Then presently he knew

’Twas cavalry that made the hillside blue﻿—

The cavalry from Wallace!

===Chinese===

装出毫无惧色来给众人打气。
可当第八个黎明来到，他害怕起
自己的软弱。他执拗地坐着，
痛苦的脸半掩在帽檐下，
假装读一本在行李里
翻出的小说。可那些铅字转着圈，
怎么摆弄都不肯说话。
终于，第九天的早晨烧起来了。
他又一次想把成群跳舞的
字母整队成行进的字，
突然间全队人疯了。
他们喊叫；照着字母的样子跳舞；
他们把帽子抛上天。
随后他明白了：是骑兵把山坡染蓝了——
从华莱士来的骑兵！

## VIII The Yellow God / 八 黄色之神

## VIII The Yellow God /

## VIII The Yellow God /

## VIII The Yellow God /

## VIII The Yellow God /

## VIII The Yellow God /

## VIII The Yellow God /

## VIII The Yellow God /

## VIII The Yellow God / 
===Original===

Autumn’s goad

Had thronged the weed-grown Powder River Road

With bison following the shrinking green.

Again the Platte and Smoky Hill had seen

The myriads nosing at the dusty hem

Of Summer’s robe; and, drifting after them,

The wild marauders vanished. Winter came;

And lo! the homesteads echoed with a name

That was a ballad sung, a saga told;

For, once men heard it, somehow it was old

With Time’s rich hoarding and the bardic lyres.

By night the settlers hugged their cowchip fires

And talked of Custer, while the children heard

The way the wild wind dramatized the word

With men and horses roaring to the fight

And valiant bugles crying down the night,

Far-blown from Cedar Creek or Fisher’s Hill.

And in their sleep they saw him riding still,

A part of all things wonderful and past,

His bright hair streaming in the battle blast

Above a surf of sabres! Roofs of shale

And soddy walls seemed safer for the tale,

The prairie kinder for that name of awe.

For now the Battle of the Washita

Was fought at every hearthstone in the land.

’Twas song to talk of Custer and his band:

The blizzard dawn, the march from Camp Supply,

Blind daring with the compass for an eye

To pierce the writhing haze; the icy fords,

The freezing sleeps; the finding of the hordes

That deemed the bitter weather and the snows

Their safety﻿—Kiowas, Arapahoes,

Cheyennes, Comanches﻿—miles of river flat

One village; Custer crouching like a cat

Among the drifts; the numbing lapse of night;

The brass band blaring in the first wan light,

The cheers, the neighing, and the wild swoop down

To widow-making in a panic town

Of widow-makers! O ’twas song to say

How Old Black Kettle paid his life that day

For bloody dawns of terror! Lyric words

Dwelt long upon his slaughtered pony herds,

His lodges burning for the roofs that blazed

That dreadful year! Rejoicing Kansas raised

Her eyes beyond the days of her defeat

And saw her hills made mighty with the wheat,

The tasselled com ranks marching on the plain;

The wonder-working of the sun and rain

And faith and labor; plenty out of dearth;

Man’s mystic marriage with the virgin Earth,

A hard-won bride.
And April came anew;

But there were those﻿—and they were human too﻿—

For whom the memory of other springs

Sought vainly in the growing dusk of things

The ancient joy. Along the Smoky Hill

===Chinese===

秋天的赶棒
把野牛又轰上了荒草蔓生的波德河大道，
跟着萎缩的绿走。普拉特和烟山
又一次看见万千兽群
嗅着夏天长袍的尘土镶边；
随之漂荡，野性的劫掠者们
消失了。冬天来临；看哪！
宅地里回响着一个名字，
它是一支唱开的歌谣、一部讲开的萨迦；
因为人们一旦听见，不知怎么它就旧了，
带着时间的丰厚收藏和游吟的琴音。

夜里，屯垦者们偎着牛饼火
谈论卡斯特，孩子们听着
野风把这个字眼搬演成戏：
人喊马嘶地冲向战斗，
勇敢的军号哭喊着压过黑夜，
从遥远的雪松溪或费舍尔山吹来。
睡梦里他们仍看见他纵马驰骋，
属于一切奇妙与既往的一部分，
明亮的头发在战斗的疾风里
飘在一片马刀的浪涛之上！
页岩的屋顶和草泥的墙
因为这个故事更觉安稳，
草原因为那个可畏的名字而更觉亲切。

如今瓦希塔之战
在全国每一块炉石上重演。
谈论卡斯特和他的队伍就是唱歌：
暴风雪的黎明、从补给营的进军，
拿罗盘当眼睛的盲勇
去穿透扭动的雪雾；冰冷的涉渡，
冻僵的露宿；发现那帮
把苦寒和风雪当作
安全的部落——基奥瓦、阿拉帕霍、
夏延、科曼切——几英里的河谷平川
一个村子；卡斯特像猫一样
伏在雪堆间；冻麻的长夜；
铜管乐队在第一缕苍白晨光里高奏，
欢呼、马嘶，随后狂暴地俯冲而下，
在寡妇制造者们惊惶的镇子里
制造寡妇！

哦，唱一唱老黑壶那天
怎样用命抵偿了
血腥的恐怖黎明，这就是歌！
抒情的字句久久流连于他遭屠的马群、
他焚烧的帐篷——抵偿那可怕的一年里
焚烧的屋顶！欢庆的堪萨斯
抬起眼睛，越过她挫败的日子，
看见自己的山丘因小麦而雄壮，
带缨的玉米列队行进在平原上；
太阳与雨水、信仰与劳作
创造的奇迹；荒歉中长出丰饶；
人与处女地的神秘婚配，
一位苦求得来的新娘。
四月又来了；
可也有一些人——他们也一样是人——
对他们来说，别的春天的记忆
在事物渐深的暮色里再也
找不回古老的欢乐。沿着烟山

===Original===

The might they could no longer hope to kill

Brawled west again, where maniacs of toil

Were chaining down the violated soil,

And plows went wiving in the bison range,

An alien-childed mother growing strange

With younger loves. May deepened in the sloughs

When down the prairie swept the wonder news

Of what had happened at the Great Salt Lake,

And how, at last, the crawling iron snake

Along the Platte had lengthened to the sea.

So shadows of a thing that was to be

Grew darker in the land.
Four years went by,

And still the solemn music of a lie

Kept peace in all the country of the Sioux.

Unharried yonder, still the bison knew

The meadows of Absoraka and throve;

But now no more the Hoary Herdsman drove

His countless cattle past the great Platte road.

Still honoring the treaty, water flowed,

And grass grew, faithful to the plighted word.

Then yonder on the Yellowstone was heard

The clank of sabers; and the Red Men saw

How Yellow Hair, the Wolf of Washita,

Went spying with his pack along the stream,

While others, bitten with a crazy dream,

Were driving stakes and peeping up the flat.

Just so it was that summer on the Platte

Before the evil came. And devil boats

Came up with stinking thunder in their throats

To scare the elk and make the bison shy.

So there was fighting yonder where the lie

Was singing flat; though nothing came of it.
And once again the stunted oaks were lit,

And down across the prairie howled the cold;

And spring came back, exactly as of old,

To resurrect the waters and the grass.

The summer deepened peacefully﻿—alas,

The last of happy summers, cherished long

As Sorrow hoards the wreckage of a song

Whose wounding lilt is dearer for the wound.

The children laughed; contented mothers crooned

About their lodges. Nothing was afraid.

The warriors talked of hunting, in the shade,

Or romped with crowing babies on their backs.

The meat was plenty on the drying racks;

The luscious valleys made the ponies glad;

And travellers knew nothing that was bad

To tell of any village they had known.

No white men yonder on the Yellowstone,

Nor any sign of trouble anywhere!
Then once again the name of Yellow Hair

Was heard with dread; for Summer, turning brown,

Beheld him lead a thousand horsemen down

To pierce the Hills where Inyan Kara towers,

Brawl southward through that paradise of flowers

===Chinese===

他们再不指望杀灭的那种伟力
又在西边喧哗，劳作的狂人们
正把遭强暴的土地用锁链锁住，
犁在野牛的地界里娶妻，
一位生了异族儿女的母亲
因新欢而变得陌生。五月
在洼沼里转深，这时惊人的消息
扫过草原：大盐湖边出了事，
那条沿普拉特河爬行的铁蛇
终于伸长到了海边。
于是一件将成之事的影子
在大地上越变越黑。

四年过去，
谎言庄严的音乐仍维持着
整个苏族土地的和平。
那边无人惊扰，野牛仍认得
阿布萨罗卡的草场，养得肥壮；
可如今灰白的牧人再不把
他数不清的牛群赶过大普拉特道。
水流着守约，
草长着，忠于许下的字。

随后黄石河那边响起了
马刀的铿锵；红种人看见
黄毛——瓦希塔之狼——
怎样带着他的狼群沿河刺探，
另一些人被疯梦咬着，
在打桩、窥探着平川。
当年普拉特河边的那个夏天，
大祸来前正是这样。魔鬼船
顶着喉咙里发臭的雷声开上来，
吓麋鹿、惊野牛。
谎言唱走了调的地方，那边就起了战事；
虽然没闹出什么名堂。矮橡又一次
被点亮，寒冷吼过草原；
春天回来，一如往昔，
复活了水与草。

夏天宁静地转深——唉，
最后一个快乐的夏天，被人久久珍藏，
像悲伤的人珍藏一支歌的残骸，
它伤人的轻快因伤而更可珍。
孩子们笑；满足的母亲们
在帐篷边哼唱。什么都不怕。
战士们在荫凉里谈打猎，
或驮着咯咯笑的婴孩嬉闹。
晾架上肉多得很；
丰美的山谷叫小马欢快；
旅人们说不出任何
他们到过的村子有什么坏话。
黄石河边没有白人，
哪儿也没有麻烦的影子！
随后黄毛的名字又一次
令人心惊地传来；因为夏天，
转褐的时候，看见他率一千骑
下来，凿进英岩峰耸立的黑山，
吵嚷着向南穿过那片花、鹿
和歌唱的溪河的乐园

===Original===

And deer and singing streams to Frenchman Creek;

Beheld him even climbing Harney Peak

To spy the land, as who should say him no!

Had grasses failed? Had water ceased to flow?

Were pledges wind?
Now scarce the sloughs were sere

When Custer, crying in the wide world’s ear

What every need and greed could understand,

Made all men see the Black Hills wonderland

Where Fortune waited, ready with a bow.

What fertile valleys pining for the plow!

What lofty forests given to the birds,

What luscious cattle pastures to the herds

Of elk and deer! What flower-enchanted parks,

Now lonely with the quails and meadowlarks,

Awaited men beneath the shielding peaks!

And in the creeks﻿—in all the crystal creeks﻿—

The blessèd creeks﻿—O wonder to behold!﻿—

Free gold﻿—the god of rabbles﻿—holy gold﻿—

And gold in plenty from the grass-roots down!
The Black Hills Country! Heard in every town,

That incantation of a wizard horn

Wrought madness. Farmers caught it in the corn

To shuck no more. No glory of the sward

Outdazzled yonder epiphanic Lord﻿—

The only revelation that was sure!

And through the cities went the singing lure,

Where drearily the human welter squirms

Like worms that lick the slime of other worms

That all may flourish. Squalor saw the gleam,

And paupers mounted in a splendid dream

The backs of luckless men, for now the weak

Inherited the earth! The fat, the sleek

Envisaged that apocalypse, and saw

Obesity to put the cringe of awe

In knees of leanness!
Sell the family cow!

Go pawn the homestead! Life was knocking now!

There might not ever be another knock.

Bring forth the hoarding of the hidden sock,

Poor coppers from the dear dead eyes of Joy!

Go seek the god that weighs the soul by troy;

Be saved, and let the devil take the rest!

The West﻿—the golden West﻿—the siren West﻿—

Behold the rainbow’s end among her peaks!

For in the creeks﻿—in all the crystal creeks﻿—

The blessèd creeks﻿—!
So wrought the rueful dream.

Chinooks of hope fed full the human stream,

Brief thawings of perennial despair.

And steadily the man-flood deepened there

With every moon along the Sioux frontier,

Where still the treaty held﻿—a rotten wier

Already trickling with a leak of men.

And some of those came drifting back again,

Transfigured palmers from the Holy Lands,

With true salvation gleaming in their hands

===Chinese===

直到法兰士曼溪；看见他
竟登上哈尼峰
去窥测这片土地，谁又拦得住他！
草衰了吗？水断流了吗？
誓约是风吗？
洼沼刚见枯色，卡斯特
就把人人听得懂的
需求和贪心的话喊进大世界的耳朵，
让所有人都看见了黑山仙乡，
命运女神正带着弓在那里等候。
多少盼犁的肥美山谷！
多少赏给飞鸟的高峻森林，
多少赏给麋群鹿群的
丰美牧场！多少花所蛊惑的园囿，
如今只有鹌鹑和草地鹨相伴，
在遮护的峰峦下等着人！
还有溪里——所有清澈的溪里——
蒙福的溪里——哦，旷世奇观！——
有自由的黄金——群氓之神——神圣的黄金——
从草根往下处处都是金！
黑山宝地！这话在每个城镇响起，
巫号的咒语
煽起了疯狂。农夫在玉米地里
听见了，玉米再也不剥了。
草原再高的光彩也压不过
那位显圣的主——
唯一靠得住的启示！

歌唱的诱饵穿城而过，
那里，人海的翻腾阴郁地蠕动，
像舔着别的虫的黏液的虫，
好叫大家都兴旺。贫贱看见了光，
穷人们骑上倒霉人的背，
进了一场堂皇的梦，因为如今
软弱的承受了地土！肥胖的、油光的
憧憬着那个启示录，看见
一身肥肉足以让瘦子的膝盖
打颤发怵！
卖掉家里的奶牛！
把宅地典了吧！命运在敲门了！
也许再不会有第二回敲门。
掏出袜子里藏的积蓄，
那些从欢乐亲爱的死眼睛里
抠出的可怜铜板！
去找那位用金衡称灵魂的神；
得了救，其余的都交给魔鬼！
西部——金色的西部——海妖的西部——
看哪，彩虹的尽头就在她的峰间！
因为溪里——所有清澈的溪里——
蒙福的溪里——！
这场悲切的梦就这么造就。
希望的奇努克风灌满了人潮，
常年绝望里短暂的解冻。
于是人的洪流沿着苏族边地
一月比一月深，
条约还立在那儿——一座烂堰，
早就渗漏着人的滴流。
有人又漂了回来，
从圣地回来的变了容的朝圣者，
手里闪着真正的救恩

===Original===

Now cleansed of labor. Thus the wonder grew.
And there were flinty hearts among the Sioux

That fall and winter. Childish, heathen folk,

Their god was but a spirit to invoke

Among the hushes of a lonely hill;

An awfulness when winter nights were still;

A mystery, a yearning to be felt

When birds returned and snow began to melt

And miracles were doing in the grass.

Negotiable Divinity, alas,

They had not yet the saving grace to know!
Nor did the hard hearts soften with the snow,

When from the high gray wilderness of rain

Johannine voices of the goose and crane

Foretold the Coming to a world enthralled;

For still along the teeming border brawled

The ever growing menace.
Summer bloomed;

But many, with the prescience of the doomed,

Could feel the shaping of the end of things

In all that gladness. How the robin sings

The sweeter in the ghastly calm that aches

With beauty lost, before the cyclone breaks!

And helpless watchers feel it as a pang,

Because of all the times the robin sang

Scarce noted in the melody of then.

About the lodges gray and toothless men

Bemoaned the larger time when life was good.

*Hey-hey*, what warriors then, what hardihood!

What terror of the Sioux among their foes!

What giants, gone, alas, these many snows﻿—

And they who knew so near their taking off!

Now beggars at the Great White Father’s trough

Forgot the bow and waited to be swilled.

The woman-hearted god the White Man killed

Bewitched the people more with every moon.

The buffalo would join the fathers soon.

The world was withered like a man grown old.

A few more grasses, and the Sioux would hold

A little paper, dirtied with a lie,

For all that used to be. ’Twas time to die.

*Hey-hey*, the braver days when life was new!
But there were strong hearts yet among the Sioux

Despite the mumbling of the withered gums.

That summer young men chanted to the drums

Of mighty deeds; and many went that fall

Where Crazy Horse and Sitting Bull and Gall

Were shepherding their people on the Tongue

And Powder yet, as when the world was young,

Contemptuous of alien ways and gods.
Now when the candles of the goldenrods

Were guttering about the summer’s bier,

And unforgetting days were hushed to hear

Some rumor of a lone belated bird,

It came to pass the Great White Father’s word

Assembled many on the White to meet

===Chinese===

如今洗净了劳苦。奇迹就这样长大。
那个秋天和冬天，苏族里
有一些燧石般的心。孩子气的异教之民，
他们的神不过是孤山
静默里可以祈请的一个灵；
是冬夜寂静时的一种可畏；
是一个奥秘，是雁归雪融、
草里行着奇迹时
一种渴望被感到的东西。
可流通的神性，唉，
他们还没那份得救的恩典去认识！
坚硬的心也没有随雪软化，
当雁和鹤从灰色的高天雨荒里
发出约翰般的声音，
向一个被慑服的世界预告降临；
因为多产的前线仍吵嚷着
越长越大的威胁。
夏天开了花；
可许多像被判决者那样有预感的人，
在一切欢欣里感到
末日的成形。知更鸟唱得
多甜啊，在龙卷风袭来前、
痛惜着失落之美的骇人宁静里！
而无助的守望者感到一阵刺痛，
因为从前知更鸟唱过那么多次，
在当时的种种旋律里
几乎从没被留意。

帐篷边，灰发落齿的老人们
哀叹生命还好的大时代。
*嘿-嘿*，那时的战士，那时的豪勇！
苏族在仇家心里是何等的恐怖！
何等的巨人，唉，去了这许多个雪季——
他们自己也知道大限已近！
如今在大白父的食槽边讨饭，
忘了弓，等着被倒进泔水。
白人杀掉的那位女人心肠的神
一月比一月更蛊惑着人们。
野牛快要到祖先那儿去了。
世界像老人一样枯了。
再几茬草，苏族手里就只剩
一张被谎言弄脏的小纸片，
抵偿往昔的一切。是时候死了。
*嘿-嘿*，生命崭新时那些更勇的日子！
可苏族里仍有坚强的心，
不管枯槁的牙床怎样咕哝。
那年夏天，年轻人们和着鼓声
唱颂伟大的功业；许多人那年秋天
去了疯马、坐牛和加尔
还在汤河与波德河边
牧养自己人民的地方，
一如世界年轻时那样，
蔑视外族的方式和神明。
当秋麒麟的烛火
在夏天的灵前将烬，
不忘的日子静下来听
一只孤单的迟鸟的传闻时，
大白父的话把许多人
召到白河去见
长刀的酋长们。

===Original===

The Long Knife chieftains. Bitter words and sweet

Grew rankly there; and stubbornly the wills

Of children met the hagglers for the Hills,

The lust for gold begetting lust for gold.

The young moon grew and withered and was old,

And still the latest word was like the first.
Then talking ended and the man-dam burst

To loose the living flood upon the West.

All winter long it deepened, and the crest

Came booming with the February thaw.

The torrent setting in through Omaha

Ground many a grist of greed, and loud Cheyenne

Became a tail-race running mules and men

Hell-bent for Eldorado. Yankton vied

With Sidney in the combing of the tide

For costly wreckage. Giddily it swirled

Where Custer City shouted to the world

And Deadwood was a howl, and Nigger Hill

A cry from Pisgah. Unabated still,

Innumerable distant freshets flowed.

The bison trail became a rutted road

And prairie schooners cruised the rolling Spring.

In labor with a monstrous farrowing,

The river packets grunted; and the plains

Were startled at the spawning of the trains

Along the Platte.
So, bitten by the imp

Of much-for-nought, the gambler and the pimp,

The hero and the coward and the fool,

The pious reader of the golden rule

By decimals, the dandy and the gawk,

The human eagle and the wingless hawk

Alert for prey, the graybeard and the lad,

The murderer, the errant Galahad,

Mistaken in the color of the gleam﻿—

All dreamers of the old pathetic dream﻿—

Pursued what no pursuing overtakes.

===Chinese===

苦话和甜话
在那里疯长；孩子们的意志
顽固地顶着争要黑山的讨价者，
对金的贪欲生出对金的贪欲。
新月长了、瘦了、老了，
最后的话还跟第一句一样。
随后谈判完了，人的堤坝决开，
把活的洪水泄向西部。
一冬它都在加深，浪头
随二月的解冻轰隆而至。
经奥马哈灌进来的激流
磨了一茬又一茬贪婪的谷子，喧闹的夏延
成了一条往下游跑骡子跑人、
亡命奔黄金国的尾水道。扬克顿
同西德尼争梳着潮水，
打捞值钱的漂浮物。它晕眩地打着旋，
卡斯特城在那里向世界叫喊，
德德伍德是一声嚎，尼格罗山
是毗斯迦山上的一声呼喊。仍未止歇，
数不清的远方支流不断汇来。
野牛的旧径成了车辙的大道，
草原的大帆船巡航在起伏的春色里。
怀着一场怪诞的产仔，
河上的明轮船哼哧；平原
被普拉特河边列车撒开的鱼汛惊动。

就这样，被那个
劳而无获的小鬼咬过的人——赌徒和皮条客、
英雄和懦夫和傻子、
用小数点读金律的
虔人、花花公子和乡巴佬、
人的鹰和没有翅膀、
却盯着猎物的鹰、老汉和少年、
杀人犯和游侠的加拉哈德——
认错了闪光颜色的人——
全都做着那古老可悯的梦——
追逐着任何追逐都追不上的东西。

## IX The Village of Crazy Horse / 九 疯马的村子

## IX The Village of Crazy Horse /

## IX The Village of Crazy Horse /

## IX The Village of Crazy Horse /

## IX The Village of Crazy Horse /

## IX The Village of Crazy Horse /

## IX The Village of Crazy Horse /

## IX The Village of Crazy Horse / 
===Original===

Meanwhile among the Powder River breaks,

Where cottonwoods and plums and stunted oaks

Made snug his village of a hundred smokes,

Young Crazy Horse was waiting for the spring.

Well found his people were in everything

That makes a winter good. But more than food

And shelter from the hostile solitude

Sustained them yonder when the sun fled far

And rustling ghost-lights capered round the Star

And moons were icy and the blue snow whined;

Or when for days the world went blizzard blind

And devils of the North came howling down.

For something holy moved about the town

With Crazy Horse.
No chieftainship had run,

Long cherished in the blood of sire and son,

To clothe him with the might he wielded then.

The Ogalalas boasted taller men

But few of fairer body. One might look

And think of water running in a brook

Or maybe of a slender hickory tree;

And something in his face might make one see

A flinty shaft-head very keen to go,

Because a hero’s hand is on the bow,

His eye upon the mark. But nothing seen

About his goodly making or his mien

Explained the man; and other men were bold.

Unnumbered were the stories that were told

(And still the legend glorified the truth)

About his war-fond, pony-taming youth

When Hump the Elder was a man to fear;

And where one went, the other would be near,

For there was love between the man and lad.

And it was good to tell what fights they had

With roving bands of Utes or Snakes or Crows.

And now that Hump was gone these many snows,

His prowess lingered. So the legend ran.

But neither Hump nor any other man

Could give the gift that was a riddle still.

What lonely vigils on a starry hill,

What fasting in the time when boyhood dies

Had put the distant seeing in his eyes,

The power in his silence? What had taught

That getting is a game that profits naught

And giving is a high heroic deed?

His plenty never neighbored with a need

Among his band. A good tough horse to ride,

The gear of war, and some great dream inside

Were Crazy Horse’s wealth. It seemed the dim

And larger past had wandered back in him

To shield his people in the days of wrong.

His thirty years were like a brave old song

That men remember and the women croon

To make their babies brave.
Now when the moon

Had wearied of December and was gone,

And bitterly the blizzard time came on,

The Great White Father had a word to say.

===Chinese===

与此同时，在波德河的断谷间，
三角叶杨、李树和矮橡
把他那百烟炊炊的村子藏得暖暖和和，
年轻的疯马正等着春天。
他的人民过冬要用的每样东西
都备得充足。可支撑那边他们
熬过太阳远遁、鬼光绕着
极星跳舞、月亮结冰、蓝雪
哀号的日子的，不止是食物
和抵御敌意荒寂的遮蔽。
因为有一样神圣的东西
随疯马在村子里走动。

没有哪一支酋长世系——
那种父子血脉里长期珍藏的——
赋予过他当时挥使的威权。
奥加拉拉人以高个子自夸，
却少有比他身段更好的。看他一眼
会想起溪里流动的水，
或者一株颀长的山核桃树；
他脸上的某种东西会叫人看见
一支急着离弦的燧石箭头，
因为一位英雄的手正扣着弓，
眼睛盯着靶子。可他的
堂堂身量和气度里没有任何
一处能解释这个人；何况别人也勇。
讲他好战驯马的少年时代的
故事数也数不清
（传说至今还在给事实镀金），
那时老驼峰是个令人畏惧的人；
一个去哪儿，另一个必在近旁，
因为这一老一少之间有爱。
讲他们怎样同漂泊的尤特人、
肖肖尼人或乌鸦人开战，最是带动。
如今驼峰去了这许多个雪季，
他的勇武还留着。传说是这么讲的。
可驼峰也好、别的什么人也好，
都给不了那件至今是谜的天赋。
星星的山上怎样的孤守，
少年将尽的时节怎样的斋戒，
把遥视放进他的眼睛、
把力量放进他的沉默？是什么教他
懂得索取是一场无益的游戏、
给予才是崇高的英雄之举？
他的富足从不曾挨着他部落里的
匮乏。一匹好骑的硬实马、
作战的装具、胸中的某个大梦，
就是疯马的全部家当。仿佛那
更大的朦胧过去退回到他身上，
来庇护他的人民于屈辱的日子。
他的三十年像一支豪迈的老歌，
男人们记得，女人们哼唱，
好叫她们的婴孩勇敢。
当十二月过腻、月亮隐去，
暴风雪的时节苦苦逼来时，
大白父有话要说。

===Original===

The frost-bit runners rode a weary way

To bring the word, and this is what it said:

“All bands, before another moon is dead,

Must gather at the agencies or share

The fate of hostiles.” Grandly unaware

Of aught but its own majesty and awe,

The big word blustered. Yet the people saw

The snow-sift snaking in the grasses, heard

The Northwind bellow louder than the word

To make them shudder with the winter fear.

“You see that there are many children here,”

Said Crazy Horse. “Our herd is getting lean.

We can not go until the grass is green.

It is a very foolish thing you say.”

And so the surly runners rode away

And Crazy Horse’s people stayed at home.
And often were the days a howling gloam

Between two howling darks; nor could one tell

When morning broke and when the long night fell;

For ’twas a winter such as old men cite

To overawe and set the youngsters right

With proper veneration for the old.

The ponies huddled humpbacked in the cold

And, dog-like, gnawed the bark of cottonwood.

But where the cuddled rawhide lodges stood

Men laughed and yarned and let the blizzard roar,

Unwitting how the tale the runners bore

Prepared the day of sorrow.
March boomed in,

And still the people revelled in their sin

Nor thought of woe already on the way.

Then, when the night was longer than the day

By just about an old man’s wink and nod,

As sudden as the storied wrath of God,

And scarce more human, retribution came.
The moony wind that night was like a flame

To sear whatever naked flesh it kissed.

The dry snow powder coiled and struck and hissed

Among the lodges. Haloes mocked the moon.

The boldest tale was given over soon

For kinder evenings; and the dogs were still

Before the prowling foe no pack might kill,

The subtle fang that feared not any fang.

But ever nearer, nearer, shod hoofs rang

To southward, unsuspected in the town.

Three cavalry battalions, flowing down

The rugged canyon bed of Otter Creek

With Reynolds, clattered out across the bleak

High prairie, eerie in the fitful light,

Where ghostly squadrons howled along the night,

Their stinging sabers gleaming in the wind.

All night they sought the village that had sinned

Yet slept the sleep of virtue, unafraid.

The Bear swung round; the stars began to fade;

The low moon stared. Then, floating in the puffs

Of wind-whipped snow, the Powder River bluffs

===Chinese===

冻伤的信使骑了困乏的一路
把话送到，话是这么说的：
“所有部落，在又一个月圆之前，
必须聚集到代办机构，否则就与
敌对分子同运。”那大话
只顾着自己的威严与慑人，
堂皇地咆哮。可人们看见
雪末在草里游成蛇，听见
北风吼得比那话更响，
叫他们因冬天的恐惧发抖。
“你们看，这里有这么多孩子，”
疯马说。“我们的马群瘦了。
草不绿我们走不了。
你们说的实在是一件蠢事。”
愠怒的信使们只好打马而去，
疯马的人民留在家里。
日子常常是两场咆哮的黑暗间
一段咆哮的暝色；分不清
早晨什么时候破、长夜什么时候落；
因为那是一个老人们引以
慑服后生、好叫他们
对长者怀有应有敬意的冬天。
小马在寒冷里弓着背挤作一团，
像狗一样啃三角叶杨的皮。
可在生皮帐篷偎暖的地方，
人们说笑讲古，任暴风雪吼，
不知道信使捎的话
正孕育着悲哀的一天。
三月轰隆而至，
人们还在罪里作乐，
不想灾祸已经上路。
而后，当夜只比昼
长出一个老人眨眼点头的工夫，
报应来了，像传说里
神的震怒一样突然，
也几乎一样地不近人情。
那晚月亮般的风像一团火，
舔到谁赤裸的皮肉就烧焦谁。
干的雪粉在帐篷间
盘绕、扑打、嘶嘶作响。月晕嘲弄着月亮。
最大胆的故事不久就收起来
留给更和气的夜晚；狗也不叫了，
在那游荡的、任何狗群都咬不死、
尖牙不怕一切尖牙的仇敌面前。
可钉了掌的蹄声越来越近、越近，
在南边，村里毫无察觉。
三个骑兵营顺着水獭溪
嶙峋的河床随雷诺兹泻下，
咔嗒咔嗒冲上凄凉的高原，
在明灭的光里鬼气森森，
幽灵的中队沿着夜嚎叫，
扎人的马刀在风里闪。
他们找了一夜那个有罪的、
却睡得像无罪一样不设防的村子。
大熊座转了过来；星星开始退去；
低月圆睁着眼。随后，在风抽的
雪团里漂着，波德河的断崖

===Original===

Gloomed yonder, and the scouts came back to tell

Of many sleeping lodges.
Now it fell

That when the bluffs were paling with the glow

Of dawn, and still the teepee tops below

Stood smokeless in the stupor of a dream,

A Sioux boy, strolling down the frozen stream

To find his ponies, wondered at the sound

Of many hoofs upon the frozen ground,

The swishing of the brush. He paused to think.

The herd, no doubt, was coming for a drink;

He’d have to chop a hole. And while he stood,

The spell of dawn upon him, from the wood﻿—

How queer!﻿—they issued marching four by four

As though enchanted, breasts and muzzles hoar

With frozen breath! Were all the ponies dead,

And these their taller spirits?
—Then he fled,

The frightened trees and bushes flowing dim,

The blanching bluff tops flinging back at him

His many-echoed yell. A frowsy squaw

Thrust up a lodge flap, blinked about her﻿—saw

What ailed her boy, and fell to screaming shrill.

The startled wolf-dogs, eager for a kill,

Rushed yelping from the lodges. Snapping sharp,

As ’twere a short string parting in a harp,

A frosty rifle sounded. Teepees spilled

A half clad rabble, and the valley filled

With uproar, spurting into jets of pain;

For now there swept a gust of killing rain

From where the plunging horses in a cloud

Of powder smoke bore down upon the crowd

To set it scrambling wildly for the breaks.

The waddling grandmas lost their precious aches

In terror for the young they dragged and drove;

Hysteric mothers staggered as they strove

To pack the creepers and the toddlers too;

And grandpas, not forgetting they were Sioux,

Made shift to do a little with the bows,

While stubbornly the young men after those

Retreated fighting through the lead-swept town

And up the sounding steeps.
There, looking down

Along the track of terror splotched with red

And dotted with the wounded and the dead,

They saw the blue-coats rage among their roofs,

Their homes flung down and given to the hoofs

Of desecrating wrath. And while they gazed

In helpless grief and fury, torches blazed

And teepees kindled. Casks of powder, stored

Against a doubtful future, belched and roared.

The hurtled lodge poles showered in the gloom,

And rawhide tops, like glutted bats of doom,

Sailed tumbling in the dusk of that despair.
Not long the routed warriors cowered there

Among the rocks and gullies of the steep.

===Chinese===

黑黑地立在那边，侦察兵回来
报告许多沉睡的帐篷。
恰逢
断崖泛起黎明的光，
下面帐篷的顶还
无烟地立在梦的酣沉里，
一个苏族男孩沿着冻河
溜达去找马群，奇怪
冻地上怎么有这么多蹄声、
灌木怎么沙沙作响。他停下想。
准是马群来喝水了；
他还得凿个冰窟窿。正站着，
黎明的魔咒罩着他，从林子里——
真怪！——他们四个一排地出来了，
像着了魔，胸口和口鼻
结满冻霜的呵气！马都死了吗，
这些是它们更高的魂灵？
——随后他逃了，
受惊的树和灌木模糊地涌过，
发白的崖顶把他
多重回声的喊抛还给他。
一个头发蓬乱的女人掀开
帐篷的帘，眨眼四顾——看清了
她孩子跑什么，尖声叫起来。
惊起的狼狗急着咬杀，
吠着从帐篷里冲出。一声脆响，
像琴上一根短弦绷断，
一支结霜的步枪开了口。帐篷倒出
一群衣衫不整的人，山谷里
满是喧嚣，喷出一股股疼痛；
这时一阵杀戮的骤雨扫来，
从战马人立、硝烟
成云、直压人群的地方，
把人群赶得疯抢着奔向断谷。

蹒跚的祖母们忘掉了宝贝的腰腿疼，
为着她们连拖带赶的孩子只顾恐怖；
歇斯底里的母亲们挣扎着
把爬的走的都捆上；
祖父们没忘了自己是苏族，
勉强用弓做了点什么，
而年轻人在他们身后
固执地且战且退，穿过铅雨横扫的村子，
退上轰响的陡坡。
在那里，沿着
溅红恐怖的来路、
点缀着死伤者的来路俯望，
他们看见蓝衣人在他们的屋顶间肆虐，
家被掀翻，交给
亵渎之怒的蹄子。他们正
无助地悲愤望着，火把燃起，
帐篷着了。为防
多变的将来存下的几桶火药
轰然炸响。抛起的帐篷杆
在昏暝里如雨，生皮的顶
像吃饱的厄运之蝠，
在绝望的暮色里翻滚飘飞。
溃乱的战士们没有多久
就不再蜷伏在陡坡的
乱石和冲沟间。

===Original===

The weakness of a panic-broken sleep

Wore off. Their babies whimpered in the frost.

Their herd was captured. Everything seemed lost

But life alone. It made them strong to die.

The death-song, stabbed with many a battle cry,

Blew down the flat﻿—a blizzard of a sound﻿—

And all the rocks and draws and brush around

Spat smoke and arrows in a closing ring.

There fell a sudden end of plundering.

Abruptly as they came the raiders fled,

And certain of their wounded, men have said,

Were left to learn what hells are made of wrath.
Now, gleaning in that strewn tornado path

Their dead and dying, came the mourning folk

To find a heap for home, a stinking smoke

For plenty. Senseless to the whirling snow,

About the bitter honey of their woe

They swarmed and moaned. What evil had they done?

Dear eyes, forever empty of the sun,

Stared up at them. These little faces, old

With pain, and pinched with more than winter cold﻿—

Why should they never seek the breast again?

A keening such as wakes the wolf in men

Outwailed the wind. Yet many a thrifty wife,

Long used to serve the urgencies of life

That make death seem a laggard’s impudence,

Descended in a rage of commonsense

Upon the wreck, collecting what would do

To fend the cold.
Now while the village grew,

A miracle of patches, jerry-built,

The young men, hot upon the trail of guilt

With Crazy Horse, found many a huddled stray

Forlorn along the thousand-footed way

The stolen herd had gone. And all day long

Their fury warmed them and their hearts were strong

To meet with any death a man might die;

For still they heard the wounded children cry,

The mourning of the women for the dead.

Nor did they deem that any hero led

The raiders. Surely nothing but the greed

Of terror could devour at such a speed

That pony-laming wallow, drift on drift.
The blue dusk mingled with the driven sift,

And still it seemed the trail of headlong flight

Was making for the wilderness of night

And safety. Then, a little way below

The mouth of Lodge Pole Creek, a dancing glow

Went up the bluff. Some few crept close to see,

And what they saw was listless misery

That crouched and shivered in a smudge of sage.

How well they cooled their baby-killing rage,

Those tentless men without a bite to eat!

And many, rubbing snow upon their feet,

Made faces that were better to behold

===Chinese===

惊碎的睡眠带来的软弱
褪去了。他们的婴孩在霜里啼哭。
马群被掳了。一切好像都丢了，
只剩性命。这让他们死得坚强。
死亡之歌插满战吼，
吹下平川——一场声音的暴风雪——
四周的乱石、冲沟和灌木
朝合拢的圈子吐出烟和箭。
劫掠戛然而止。
袭击者来得突然，去得也突然，
人们说，他们的某些伤号
被留下领教愤怒造的地狱。
这时，哀恸的人们在那条
龙卷风肆虐的路上
拾捡死者和垂死者，回家的
只有一堆残骸，丰盛的
只有一缕恶臭的烟。对旋雪
毫无知觉，围着他们苦难的
苦蜜，他们围聚哀号。
他们造了什么孽？
亲爱的眼睛永远空掉了太阳，
向上望着他们。这些小脸，
被疼痛催老、被比冬寒更狠的
东西掐瘦——为什么再也不找奶了？
一声哭嚎压过了风，
能把人里的狼哭醒。可许多
精明的主妇，惯于伺候
那些叫死亡显得像懒汉无礼的
生计急务，带着一股常识的火气
扑上废墟，拾捡一切还能
御寒的东西。

这时，村子渐渐
搭成一座拼凑的奇迹，
年轻人们随疯马紧蹑着
罪行的踪迹，在被掠马群
千足踏过的路上，发现许多
蜷缩掉队的散马。一整天，
怒火给他们取暖，他们的心
坚强得敢领教人所能死的任何死；
因为他们耳中还有伤孩的哭、
女人对死者的哀号。
他们也不信袭击者
有什么英雄带领。除了
恐怖的贪馋，还有什么
能那样飞快地吞完那一程
伤马的连滚带爬、一程又一程。
蓝色的黄昏混进扑面的雪末，
仓皇奔逃的踪迹仍像是奔向
夜的原野和
安全。随后，在柴杆溪口
下游一点，一道跳动的火光
升上断崖。几个人爬近去看，
看见的是无精打采的凄惨，
蜷在一蓬鼠尾草的烟里发抖。
那些没帐篷、没一口吃的的人，
杀婴之怒冷却得可真好！
许多人往脚上搓着雪，
做出的怪相倒比

===Original===

Than how their shaking horses took the cold

With tight-tailed rumps against the bitter flaw.

Beyond the camp and scattered up the draw

The hungry ponies pawed the frozen ground,

And there was no one anywhere around

To guard them. White-man medicine was weak.
Now all the young men, hearing, burned to wreak

Their hate upon the foe. A wiser will

Restrained them. “Wait a better time to kill,”

Said Crazy Horse. “Our lives are few to give

And theirs are many. Can our people live

Without the herd? We must not die today.

The time will come when I will lead the way

Where many die.”
Like hungry wolves that prowl

The melancholy marches of the owl

Where cows and calves are grazing unafraid,

The pony stalkers went. A stallion neighed,

Ears pricked to question what the dusk might bring;

Then all the others fell to whinnying

And yonder in the camp the soldiers heard.

Some rose to point where many shadows, blurred

With driven snow and twilight, topped a rise

And vanished in the smother. Jeering cries

Came struggling back and perished in the bruit

Of charging wind. No bugles of pursuit

Aroused the camp. Night howled along the slough.

===Chinese===

他们那夹紧尾巴迎着
刺骨狂风的打颤的马受冻的样子。
营外，沿着冲沟散开，
饥饿的小马刨着冻土，
四周没有任何人
看管。白人的巫术弱了。
年轻人们一听，都烧着
要把恨泄到敌人头上。一个更明智的
意志拦住了他们。“等一个更好的杀机，”
疯马说。“我们的命少，
他们的命多。没有马群，
我们的人民活得成吗？今天不能死。
时候会来，我会带路
去那死人众多的地方。”
像饿狼巡行在
夜枭管辖的凄凉边地、
母牛牛犊不设防范食的地方，
牵马的人摸了过去。一匹儿马嘶鸣，
竖起耳朵探问黄昏会带来什么；
随后所有的马都嘶鸣起来，
那边的营里士兵们听见了。
有人起身指去：许多影子，混着
扑面的雪和暮色，翻过一道
山梁，消失在迷茫里。嘲弄的呼喊
挣扎着飘回来，死在狂风
冲锋的轰鸣里。没有追击的号声
惊动那座营地。夜沿着洼沼嚎叫。

## X The Sun Dance / 十 太阳舞

## X The Sun Dance /

## X The Sun Dance /

## X The Sun Dance /

## X The Sun Dance /

## X The Sun Dance /

## X The Sun Dance / 
===Original===

Now wheresoever thawing breezes blew

And green began to prickle in the brown,

There went the tale of Crazy Horse’s town

To swell a mood already growing there.

For something more than Spring was in the air,

And, mightier than any maiden’s eyes,

The Lilith-lure of Perilous Emprise

Was setting all the young men’s blood astir.

How fair the more than woman face of her

Whose smile has gulfed how many a daring prow!

What cities burn for jewels on her brow;

Upon her lips what vintages are red!

Her lovers are the tallest of the dead

Forever. When the streams of Troas rolled

So many heroes seaward, she was old;

Yet she is young forever to the young.
’Twas now the murmur of the man-flood, flung

Upon the Hills, grew ominously loud.

The whole white world seemed lifted in a cloud

To sweep the prairie with a monstrous rain.

Slay one, and there were fifty to be slain!

Give fifty to the flame for torturing,

Then count the marching multitude of Spring

Green blade by blade!
Still wilder rumors grew;

They told of soldiers massed against the Sioux

And waiting till the grass was good, to fall

On Crazy Horse and Sitting Bull and Gall

That all the country might be safe for theft,

And nothing of a warrior race be left

But whining beggars in a feeding pen.

Alas, the rights of men﻿—of other men﻿—

That centenary season of the Free!

No doubt the situation wanted tea

To make it clear! But long before the green

Had topped the hills, the agencies grew lean

Of youth and courage. Did a watch dog bark

Midway between the owl and meadowlark?﻿—

Then other lads with bow and shield and lance

Were making for the Region of Romance

Where Sitting Bull’s weird medicine was strong

And Crazy Horse’s name was like a song

A happy warrior sings before he dies,

And Gall’s a wind of many battle cries

That flings a thousand ponies on the doomed.
So where the Powder and the Rosebud boomed,

Men met as water of the melting snows.

The North Cheyennes and North Arapahoes,

Become one people in a common cause

With Brulés, Minneconjoux, Hunkpapas,

Sans Arcs and Ogalalas, came to throng

The valleys; and the villages were long

With camp on camp. Nor was there any bluff,

In all the country, that was tall enough

To number half the ponies at a look.

Here young June came with many tales of Crook,

The Gray Fox, marching up the Bozeman Road.

===Chinese===

如今，凡有解冻的微风拂过、
绿色开始从褐色里刺出的地方，
疯马的村子的故事就传到那里，
给那里本已在长的情绪添涨。
因为空气里的不止是春天；
比任何少女的眼睛更有力的，
是那“危险功业”的莉莉斯之诱，
它让所有年轻男人的血騒动。
她那张超越女人的脸多么美，
她的笑吞没了多少条胆壮的船头！
多少座城为她眉上的珠翠焚烧；
她唇上红的是何等的陈酿！
她的情人们永远
是死者中最高的那一群。
特洛阿的河水把那么多英雄
滚向大海时，她已经老了；
可对年轻的人，她永远年轻。
这时，涌上黑山的人潮的
低语不祥地大了起来。
整个白人的世界仿佛被掀上云端，
要用一场怪雨扫荡草原。
杀一个，还有五十个等着被杀！
把五十个交给火焰去折磨，
再一叶一叶去数春天行进的
大军吧！谣言越传越野；
说士兵正聚拢对付苏族，
只等草色转好，就扑向
疯马、坐牛和加尔，
好让整片土地放心偷盗，
让武人的种族只剩下
食槽里哀叫的乞丐，别无孑遗。
唉，人的权利——别人的权利——
自由的那个百年纪念季！
毫无疑问，这情形得来点茶
才说得清！可早在绿
爬上山头之前，代办机构里
青春和胆气就流失瘦了。
看门狗在夜枭和草地鹨之间
吠一声？——随即又有别的少年
带着弓、盾和长矛奔向
传奇的地界，
那里坐牛的古怪巫术正强，
疯马的名字像一支快乐的战士
死前唱的歌，
加尔是一阵挟着千百战吼的风，
把一千匹小马掷向该死的人。
于是在波德河与玫瑰蕾河轰响的地方，
人们像融雪的水一样汇合。
北方夏延和北方阿拉帕霍，
同布鲁勒、米尼康朱、洪克帕帕、
桑萨克和奥加拉拉
因一个共同的缘由结成一族，
涌进河谷；村子一营接一营
排得老长。全国上下，
没有一道断崖高得
能一眼数清一半的小马。
六月在这里来时，带着许多
“灰狐”克鲁克的传闻——
他正沿博兹曼小道开上来。

===Original===

How long a dust above his horsemen flowed!

How long a dust his walking soldiers made!

What screaming thunder when the pack-mules brayed

And all the six-mule wagon teams replied!

The popping of the whips on sweaty hide,

How like a battle when the foe is bold!

And from the North still other tales were told

By those who heard the steamboats wheeze and groan

With stuffs of war along the Yellowstone

To feed the camps already waiting there.

Awaiting what? The might of Yellow Hair

Now coming from the Heart’s mouth! Rumor guessed

How many Snakes were riding from the West

To join the Whites against their ancient foes;

How many Rees, how many of the Crows

Remembered to be jealous of the Sioux.

Look north, look south﻿—the cloud of trouble grew.

Look east, look west﻿—the whole horizon frowned.

But it was better to be ringed around

With enemies, to battle and to fail,

Than be a beggar chief like Spotted Tail,

However fattened by a hated hand.
Now when the full moon flooded all the land

Before the laughter of the owls began,

They turned to One who, mightier than Man,

Could help them most﻿—the Spirit in the sun;

For whatsoever wonder-work is done

Upon the needy earth, he does it all.

For him the whole world sickens in the fall

When streams cease singing and the skies go gray

And trees and bushes weep their leaves away

In hopeless hushes empty of the bird,

And all day long and all night long are heard

The high geese wailing after their desire.

But, even so, his saving gift of fire

Is given unto miserable men

Until they see him face to face again

And all his magic happen, none knows how.

It was the time when he is strongest now;

And so a holy man whose heart was good

Went forth to find the sacred cottonwood

Belovèd of the Spirit. Straight and high,

A thing of worship yearning for the sky,

It flourished, sunning in a lonely draw;

And there none heard the holy man nor saw

What rites were done, save only one who knows

From whence the new moon comes and whither goes

The old, and what the stars do all day long.

Thereafter came the people with a song,

The men, the boys, the mothers and the maids,

All posy-crowns and blossom-woven braids,

As though a blooming meadow came to see.

And fruitful women danced about the tree

To make the Spirit glad; for, having known

The laughter of the children of their own,

===Chinese===

他的骑队上头流过多长的一道尘！
他的步队踩起多长的一道尘！
驮骡一叫、六骡车队齐声
应和时，那是怎样尖叫的雷！
皮鞭在汗湿的皮肉上炸响，
多像强敌当前的一场血战！
北方还传来别的传闻，
说的人听见过汽船沿黄石河
载着战争物资喘息呻吟，
去供应早已等在那边的军营。
等什么？黄毛的大军
正从“心”口开来！谣言猜着
多少肖肖尼人正从西边赶来
助白人打他们古老的仇敌；
多少里斯人、多少乌鸦人
想起了对苏族的旧妒。
看北、看南——祸云在长。
看东、看西——整个天际都皱眉。

可被仇敌四面围定、
战斗、失败，也好过
当斑尾那样的乞丐酋长，
不管被仇家的手养得多肥。
当满月照亮大地、
夜枭还未开始笑的时候，
他们求告于一位比人
更有力、最能帮他们的——太阳里的神灵；
因为在这匮乏的地上
行的一切奇迹，都是他行的。
为他，整个世界在秋天害病，
溪河停止歌唱、天空转灰，
树木灌丛在失去飞鸟的
无望静默里哭落叶子，
整日整夜只听见
高天的雁追着渴望哀鸣。
可即便如此，他救命的火
仍赐给愁苦的人们，
直到他们再与他面对面相见，
他的一切魔法不知怎么地发生。
现在正是他力量最强的时节；
于是一位心地良善的神人
出去寻找那棵神灵
所爱的圣三角叶杨。又直又高，
一件渴望着天的膜拜之物，
它在一道孤谷里向阳繁茂；
那里没有人听见神人、也没有人看见
行了什么仪轨，除了那位
知道新月从何处来、旧月
往何处去、星星白天做什么的。

随后人们唱着歌来了，
男人、男孩、母亲和少女，
满头花冠、满辫花辫，
仿佛一片开花的草地来看。
多产的妇人们绕树起舞，
讨神灵欢喜；因为她们
听过自己孩子的笑声，

===Original===

Some goodness of the earth, the giving one,

Was in them and was pleasing to the Sun,

The prairie-loving nourisher of seed.
A warrior who had done the bravest deed

Yet dared that year by any of the Sioux

Now struck the trunk as one who counts a coup

Upon a dreaded foe; and prairie gifts

He gave among the poor, for nothing lifts

The heart like giving. Let the coward save﻿—

Big hoard and little heart; but still the brave

Have more with nothing! Singing virgins came

Whose eyes had never learned to droop with shame,

Nor was there any present, man or youth,

Could say them aught of ill and say the truth,

For sweet as water in a snow-born brook

Where many birches come and lean to look

Along a mountain gorge, their spirits were.

And each one took the ax they gave to her

And smote the tree with many a lusty stroke;

And with a groan the sleeper in it ’woke

And far hills heard the falling shout of him.

Still rang the axes, cleaving twig and limb

Along the tapered beauty of the bole,

Till, naked to the light, the sacred pole

Lay waiting for the bearers.
They who bore

Were chieftains, and their fathers were before,

And all of them had fasted, as they should;

Yet none dared touch the consecrated wood

With naked fingers, out of pious fear.

And once for every season of the year

They paused along the way, remembering

With thanks alike the autumn and the spring,

The winter and the summer.
Then it fell

That many warriors, lifting up a yell

That set their ponies plunging, thundered down

Across the center of the circled town

Where presently the holy tree should stand;

For whosoever first of all the band

Could strike the sacred spot with bow or spear

Might gallop deep among the dead that year

Yet be of those whom busy Death forgot.

And sweaty battle raged about the spot

Where screaming ponies, rearing to the thrust

Of screaming ponies, clashed amid the dust,

And riders wrestled in the hoof-made gloam.
So, having safely brought the sun-tree home,

The people feasted as for victory.
And on the second day they dressed the tree

And planted it with sacred songs and vows,

And round it reared a wall of woven boughs

That opened to the mystic source of day.

And with the next dawn mothers came to lay

Their babies down before the holy one,

Each coveting a hero for a son

Or sturdy daughters fit to nurse the bold.

===Chinese===

大地、那给予者的某种善
就在她们身上，也讨太阳喜欢——
草原之爱的种子的哺育者。
一位立下那年苏族
任何人所敢立的最勇功绩的战士
照着数记一个可畏仇敌
战功的样子击了树干；
他把草原的礼物散给穷人，
因为没有什么比给予
更提得起心。让懦夫去攒——
攒多藏多心眼小；而勇者
一无所有反而更富！
唱着歌的少女们来了，
她们的眼睛从没学会羞惭地垂下，
在场的无论男人少年，
谁能说她们半句坏话而又属实；
因为她们的性子甜得
像雪生的溪水——许多白桦
凑过来俯身照影的
一道山涧。每人接过
递来的斧子，
一下一下结实地砍那棵树；
树里的沉睡者呻吟一声
醒了，远山听见他倒下的欢呼。
斧声不停，顺着树干
渐细的美，劈开枝桠，
直到圣树赤裸在光里，
躺着等抬它的人。
抬的人
是酋长，他们的父亲从前也是，
人人都按规矩斋戒过；
可出于虔敬的畏惧，
谁也不敢用赤裸的手指
碰那献祭过的木头。
一年每有一个季节，
他们就在路上停一停，
怀着同样的感谢
记起秋和春、
冬和夏。
恰在这时，
许多战士发一声喊，
惊得小马腾跳，雷鸣着
冲过环形村镇的正中——
圣树马上就要立在那里；
因为全队谁最先
用弓或矛击中那个圣点，
谁就能在那一年深入
死人堆里疾驰，
却仍是被忙碌的死神
忘掉的人。
汗湿的战斗在那个点周围
狂烧，尖叫的小马迎着
尖叫的小马的突刺，
在尘土里撞在一起，
骑手在蹄子搅起的昏暝里角力。
就这样，太阳树平安到家，
人们像打了胜仗一样宴乐。
第二天他们给树着装，
用圣歌和誓愿把它栽立，
绕着它编起一道树枝的墙，
向着白日神秘的源头敞开。
又次日黎明，母亲们来把
婴孩放在圣者面前，
个个盼着得一个英雄的儿子，
或养得活勇者的壮实女儿。

===Original===

Then when the fourth dawn came the war drums rolled;

And from their lodges, lean and rendered pure

With meatless days, those vowing to endure

The death-in-torture to be born again,

Came naked there before the holy men

Who painted them with consecrated paint.

And if a knee seemed loosened, it was faint

With fast and weary vigil, not with dread;

For lo! the multitudinary dead

Pressed round to see if heroes such as they

Still walked the earth despite the smaller day

When ’twas not half so easy to be brave.

Now, prone beneath the pole, as in a grave,

Without a wince each vower took the blade

In chest or back, and through the wound it made

Endured the passing of the rawhide thong,

Swung from the pole’s top; raised a battle song

To daunt his anguish; staggered to his feet

And, leaning, capered to the war drum’s beat

A dizzy rigadoon with Agony.
So all day long the spirit-haunted tree

Bore bloody fruitage, groaning to the strain,

For with the dropping of the ripe-in-pain,

Upon the stem the green-in-courage grew.

And seldom had there fallen on the Sioux

So great a wind of ghostly might as then.

Boys tripped it, bleeding, with the tortured men.

The mothers, daughters, sisters, sweethearts, wives

Of those who suffered, gashed their flesh with knives

To share a little of the loved one’s pang;

And all day long the sunning valley rang

With songs of courage; and the mother sod

Received the red libation; and the god

Gave power to his people.

===Chinese===

然后，第四个黎明来到，战鼓擂响；
那些许愿要受酷刑之死、
以求重生的人，从帐篷里
精瘦着、被无肉的日子
涤净着出来，赤裸着
站到圣者面前，
圣者用献祭的颜料给他们涂身。
若有谁的膝盖显得发软，
那也是斋戒和疲惫的
守夜熬虚的，不是吓的；
因为看哪！大批的亡魂
挤上来看这样的英雄
是否还行走于世，尽管这
变小了的日子、这当勇士
不及从前一半容易的日子。
俯卧在树下，像在墓穴里，
每个许愿者都毫不畏缩地
让刀刃穿进胸口或背，
忍着刀口里穿过
生皮绳——绳头从杆顶垂下；
唱起战歌
镇住自己的痛楚；挣扎着站起，
斜着身子，应着战鼓
同“苦痛”跳一支晕眩的里戈东。
就这样，鬼魂萦绕的树
一整天结着血的果子，在重压下呻吟，
因为痛熟了的果子落下去，
勇气青着的又在蒂上长出来。
苏族身上很少
刮过那时那样大的一阵
鬼魂伟力之风。
男孩们流着血同受刑的
男人们一起踏着节拍。
受难者的母亲、女儿、姐妹、
情人和妻子用刀割开
自己的皮肉，
分尝一点亲爱者的痛；
一整天，晒着太阳的山谷
回响着勇气的歌；母亲的草土
接住红色的奠酒；神
把力量给了他的人民。

## XI The Seventh Marches / 十一 第七骑兵团开拔

## XI The Seventh Marches /

## XI The Seventh Marches /

## XI The Seventh Marches /

## XI The Seventh Marches /

## XI The Seventh Marches / 
===Original===

Far away,

One foggy morning in the midst of May,

Fort Lincoln had beheld the marshalling

Of Terry’s forces; heard the bugle sing,

The blaring of the band, the brave hurrah

Of Custer’s men recalling Washita

And confident of yet another soon.

How gallantly in column of platoon

(So many doomed and given to the ghost)

Before the weeping women of the post

They sat their dancing horses on parade!

What made the silence suddenly afraid

When, with a brazen crash, the band went whist

And, dimmer in the clinging river mist,

The line swung westward? Did the Ree squaws know,

Through some wise terror of the ancient foe,

To what unearthly land their warriors led

The squadrons? Better suited to the dead

Than to the quick, their chanting of farewell

Grew eerie in the shadow, rose and fell﻿—

The long-drawn yammer of a lonely dog.

But when at length the sun broke through the fog,

What reassurance in the wide blue air,

The solid hills, and Custer riding there

With all the famous Seventh at his heel!

And back of those the glint of flowing steel

Above the dusty infantry; the sun’s

Young glimmer on the trundled Gatling guns;

And then the mounted Rees; and after that

The loaded pack mules straggling up the flat

And wagons crowding wagons for a mile!
What premonition of the afterwhile

Could darken eyes that saw such glory pass

When, lilting in a muffled blare of brass

Off yonder near the sundering prairie rim,

“The Girl I Left Behind Me” floated dim

As from the unrecoverable years?

And was it nothing but a freak of tears,

The vision that the grieving women saw?

For suddenly a shimmering veil of awe

Caught up the van. One could have counted ten

While Custer and the half of Custer’s men

Were riding up a shining steep of sky

As though to join the dead that do not die

But haunt some storied heaven of the bold.

And then it seemed a smoke of battle rolled

Across the picture, leaving empty air

Above the line that slowly shortened there

And dropped below the prairie and was gone.
Now day by day the column straggled on

While moody May was dribbling out in rain

To make a wagon-wallow of the plain

Between the Muddy and the upper Heart.

Where lifeless hills, as by demonic art,

Were hewn to forms of wonderment and fear,

Excited echoes flocked about to hear,

And any sound brought riotous applause,

So long among the scarps and tangled draws

===Chinese===

远远地，五月当中
一个雾蒙蒙的早晨，林肯堡
看着特里的部队结集；
听军号歌唱、
乐队高奏，卡斯特的部下
喊着英勇的瓦希塔式的欢呼，
相信不久还有一场。在哨站
垂泪的妇人面前，他们
分列纵队、稳坐腾跃的战马
接受检阅，多么英武！
（这么多人已经注定，交给了鬼魂）
乐队铿然一收，队伍
在粘滞的河雾里转暗，
向西开拔时，是什么叫寂静
突然害怕起来？里斯族的女人们
是不是凭着对古老仇敌的
某种明智的恐惧，知道她们的战士
把骑兵中队带向了哪片
非人间的土地？她们送别的吟唱
与其说配给活人，
不如说配给死人，
在阴影里变得瘆人，起了又落——
像一条独狗拖长的哀号。

可当太阳终于破开雾，
这宽阔的蓝天、坚实的山丘、
卡斯特带着赫赫有名的
第七团骑马走在这中间，多么让人心安！
他们身后，尘土飞扬的步兵
头顶闪着流动的钢光；
朝阳在转运的加特林机枪上
闪着年轻的光；
然后是骑马的里斯人；再后
是驮重的驮骡歪歪斜斜爬上平川，
还有车挤着车挤出一英里的辎重队！
看着这样的光荣经过的眼睛，
哪会有什么后来的
预感来搅暗？当远处
靠近草原断开的天边，
《我留下的姑娘》在铜管
喑哑的吹奏里轻快地飘着，淡下去，
像从那追不回的年月里飘来？
悲恸的女人们看见的幻象，
难道只是泪水的把戏？
因为突然间，一面闪颤的敬畏之纱
拢住了前队。人可以数到十，
卡斯特和卡斯特的半队人马
正沿着一段闪亮的天坡向上骑行，
仿佛去同那些不死的死者会合，
他们出没在勇者的某个传说天堂。
随后仿佛一缕战场的硝烟
滚过画面，只留下空空的空气
在那条慢慢变短的队伍上方，
沉下草原，不见了。

此后队伍一天天逶迤前行，
任性的五月沥着雨快尽了，
把泥河与上游“心”河之间的
平原泡成大车的泥沼。
在那里，死气沉沉的山丘像经了
恶魔的手艺，凿成
惊奇与恐怖的形状，
兴奋的回声成群赶来听，
任何声响都博得满堂彩，
因为寂静和寂静的魔咒
在峭壁和乱谷间盘桓得太久，

===Original===

Had clung that silence and the spell of it.

Some fiend-deserted city of the Pit

The region seemed, with crumbling domes and spires;

For still it smoked with reminiscent fires,

And in the midst, as ’twere the stream of woe,

A dark flood ran.
June blustered in with snow,

And all the seasons happened in a week.

Beyond the Beaver and O’Fallon creek

They toiled. Amid the wilderness of breaks

The drainage of the lower Powder makes,

They found a way and brought the wagons through;

Nor had they sight or sign of any Sioux

In all that land. Here Reno headed south

With packs and half the troopers for the mouth

Of Mispah, thence to scout the country west

About the Tongue; while Terry and the rest

Pushed onward to the Yellowstone to bide

With Gibbon’s men the news of Reno’s ride.
Mid June drew on. Slow days of waiting bred

Unhappy rumors. Everybody said

What no one, closely questioned, seemed to know.

Enormous numerations of the foe,

By tentative narration made exact

And tagged with all the circumstance of fact,

Discredited the neat official tale.

’Twas well when dawn came burning down the vale

And river fogs were lifting like a smoke

And bugles, singing reveille, awoke

A thousand-throated clamor in the herd.

But when the hush was like a warning word

And taps had yielded darkness to the owl,

A horse’s whinny or a kiote’s howl

Made true the wildest rumors of the noon.
So passed the fateful seventeenth of June

When none might guess how much the gossip lacked

To match the unimaginative fact

Of what the upper Rosebud saw that day:

How Crook, with Reno forty miles away,

Had met the hordes of Crazy Horse and Gall,

And all the draws belched cavalries, and all

The ridges bellowed and the river fen

Went dizzy with the press of mounted men﻿—

A slant cyclonic tangle; how the dark

Came not a whit too early, and the lark

Beheld the Gray Fox slinking back amazed

To Goose Creek; what a dust the victors raised

When through the Cherish Hills by many a pass

They crowded down upon the Greasy Grass

To swell the hostile thousands waiting there.
Alas, how wide they made for Yellow Hair

That highway leading to the shining Past!
Now came the end of waiting, for at last

The scouring squadrons, jogging from the south,

Had joined their comrades at the Rosebud’s mouth

With doubtful news. That evening by the fires,

===Chinese===

那地区像深坑里
被恶魔遗弃的城，穹顶和尖塔
都在崩塌；因为它还冒着
记忆之火的烟，正中
像一道祸水，流着黑的洪流。
六月挟着雪咆哮而至，
一个星期里过完四季。
他们苦苦跋涉过了比弗溪
和奥法伦溪。在下波德河
水系造就的断谷荒野里，
他们寻出一条路，把大车带了过去；
在那整片土地上却看不见
苏族人影、寻不着苏族痕迹。
在这里，雷诺带着驮队和
半数骑兵向南去米斯帕河口，
再往西侦察汤河一带的乡野；
特里和余部继续推进到黄石河，
同吉本的人一起等雷诺
此行的消息。六月中旬临近。
缓慢的等待日子养出
不祥的谣言。人人都在说
可细问起来谁也不懂的事。
把敌人的天文数字
用试探的叙述说成确数，
再贴上事实的全套细节，
官方那套干净的说法就没人信了。
黎明烧下河谷、河雾
像烟一样升起、号角
唱着起床号唤醒
马群千喉的喧哗时，日子还好过。
可当静默像一句警告、
熄灯号把黑暗交给夜枭时，
一声马嘶或一声郊狼的嚎
就坐实了正午最野的谣言。
决定命运的六月十七日就这么过去，
那时没人猜得到那些闲话
离上游玫瑰蕾河当天
所见的、想象不出的事实还差多远：
克鲁克——雷诺远在四十英里外——
怎样撞上疯马和加尔的大队，
所有的冲沟喷出骑兵，所有的
山脊轰鸣，河滩沼泽
被骑马的人挤得发晕——
一场斜拧的旋风般的乱战；
黑夜来得一点也不嫌早；
云雀看见灰狐惊愕地
溜回鹅溪；得胜者扬起
好大一阵尘土，当他们取道
切里什山的许多隘口
涌下油腻草滩，
去壮大等在那里的敌对千军。
唉，他们为黄毛把那条
通向闪亮往昔的大道拓得多宽！
等待结束了：南边
一路搜索的中队终于小跑着
到玫瑰蕾河口同战友会合，
带来没把握的消息。当晚在火边，

===Original===

According to their dreads or their desires,

The men discussed the story that was told

About a trail, not over three weeks old,

That led across the country from the Tongue,

Struck up the Rosebud forty miles and swung

Again to westward over the divide.

Some said, “We’ll find blue sky the other side,

Then back to Lincoln soon!” But more agreed

’Twould not be so with Custer in the lead.

“He’ll eat his horses when the hardtack’s gone

Till every man’s afoot!” And thereupon

Scarred veterans remembered other days

With Custer﻿—thirsty marches in the blaze

Of Texas suns, with stringy mule to chew;

And times when splinters of the North Pole blew

Across the lofty Colorado plains;

And muddy going in the sullen rains

Of Kansas springs, when verily you felt

Your backbone rub the buckle of your belt

Because there weren’t any mules to spare.

Aye, there were tales to make the rookies stare

Of Custer’s daring and of Custer’s luck.

And some recalled that night before they struck

Black Kettle’s village. Whew! And what a night!

A foot of snow, and not a pipe alight,

And not a fire! You didn’t dare to doze,

But kept your fingers on your horse’s nose

For fear he’d nicker and the chance be lost.

And all night long there, starry in the frost,

You’d see the steaming Colonel striding by.

And when the first light broke along the sky,

Yet not enough to make a saber shine,

You should have seen him gallop down the line

With hair astream! It warmed your blood to see

The way he clapped his hat beneath his knee

And yelled “Come on!” “Go ask him if we came!”
And so they conjured with a magic name;

But, wakeful in the darkness after taps,

How many saddened, conscious of the lapse

Of man-denying time!
The last owl ceased.

A pewee sensed the changing of the east

And fluted shyly, doubtful of the news.

A wolf, returning from an all-night cruise

Among the rabbits, topped a staring rim

And vanished. Now the cooks were stirring dim,

Waist-deep in woodsmoke crawling through the damp.

The shadow lifted from the snoring camp.

The bugle sang. The horses cried ha! ha!

The mule herd raised a woeful fanfara

To swell the music, singing out of tune.

Up came the sun.
The Seventh marched at noon,

Six hundred strong. By fours and troop by troop,

With packs between, they passed the Colonel’s group

By Terry’s tent; the Rickarees and Crows

===Chinese===

人们按各自的恐惧或心愿，
议论着传说的那条踪迹——
留下不过三星期——
从汤河那头横过乡野，
沿玫瑰蕾河上溯四十英里，
又折向西翻过分水岭。
有人说：“翻过山就是蓝天，
很快就能回林肯堡了！”
但更多人认为
有卡斯特领队，不会这么收场。
“硬饼一吃完他就吃马，
吃到人人步行！”于是
老兵们记起了跟卡斯特
走过的别的日子——得克萨斯的
烈日下渴着行军，嚼着筋多肉少的骡肉；
北极的冰碴子刮过
科罗拉多高原的日子；
堪萨斯春天阴沉的雨里
踩着泥走，走真能觉出
脊梁骨蹭着腰带扣，
因为连一头多余的骡子都没有了。
是啊，有的是叫新兵
瞪眼的故事，讲卡斯特的胆、卡斯特的运。

有人还记得袭击黑壶村子前
的那一夜。嚯！好一个夜！
一尺深的雪，没有一斗烟点着，
没有一堆火！你不敢打盹，
手指头捂着马鼻子，
怕它一嘶叫机会就丢了。
整夜霜天星斗下，
你看得见冒着热气的上校
大步走来走去。当天边
透出第一线亮、还不够照亮
一把马刀的时候，
你真该看看他沿队列
纵马疾驰、头发飘成的样子！
看他把帽子往膝下一夹、
吼一声“跟上”，你的血都热了！
“去问他咱们来没来过！”
他们就这样念动一个有魔力的名字；
可熄灯号后的黑暗里醒着的人，
多少暗暗神伤，意识到那
否定人的时间的流逝！
最后一只夜枭停了。
一只绿霸鹟觉出东方的变动，
怯怯地试了试笛音，还拿不准消息。
一只狼巡完一夜的兔子，
翻上一道凝望的山脊，
不见了。厨子们影影绰绰动起来，
没在贴地爬行的柴烟里。
影子从打着鼾的营地上撤去。
号角唱了。马群喊哈！哈！
骡群扬起一支悲惨的号角
给乐声添乱，唱得走调。
太阳上来了。
第七团正午开拔，
六百之众。四骑一排，一队接一队，
中间夹着驮队，经过特里
帐篷旁上校那一组人；里斯人和乌鸦人

===Original===

Astride their shaggy paints and calicoes;

The regimental banner and the grays;

And after them the sorrels and the bays,

The whites, the browns, the piebalds and the blacks.

One flesh they seemed with those upon their backs,

Whose weathered faces, like and fit for bronze,

Some gleam of unforgotten battle-dawns

Made bright and hard. The music of their going,

How good to hear!﻿—though mournful beyond knowing;

The low-toned chanting of the Crows and Rees,

The guidons whipping in a stiff south breeze

Prophetical of thunder-brewing weather,

The chiming spurs and bits and crooning leather,

The shoe calks clinking on the scattered stone,

And, fusing all, the rolling undertone

Of hoofs by hundreds rhythmically blent﻿—

The diapason of an instrument

Strung taut for battle music.
So they passed.

And Custer, waking from a dream at last

With still some glory of it in his eyes,

Shook hands around and said his last goodbyes

And swung a leg across his dancing bay

That champed the snaffle, keen to be away

Where all the others were. Then Gibbon spoke,

Jocosely, but with something in the joke

Of its own pleasantry incredulous:

“Now don’t be greedy, Custer! Wait for us!”

And Custer laughed and gave the bay his head.

“I won’t!” he cried. Perplexed at what he said,

They watched the glad bay smoking up the draw

And heard the lusty welcoming hurrah

That swept along the column. When it died,

The melancholy pack mules prophesied

And ghost-mules answered.

===Chinese===

骑着毛蓬蓬的花斑马和杂色马；
团旗和灰马；
随后是栗色马和枣红马，
白马、棕马、花马和黑马。
他们同背上的人仿佛一体，
那些风霜的脸，像青铜
也配得上青铜，某种
未忘的战斗黎明的光
把它们照得又亮又硬。他们开拔的
音乐多好听！——却悲得没法说；
乌鸦人和里斯人低低的吟唱，
队旗在挺硬的南风里啪啪翻卷，
预告着酝雷的天气，
马刺、衔铁叮当合鸣、皮件低吟，
蹄铁的钉掌磕着散落的石头，
而把这一切熔在一起的，
是千百马蹄合成节律的
滚动的低音——
一件绷紧了
待奏战乐的乐器的洪音。
就这样他们过去了。
卡斯特终于从一场梦里醒来，
眼里还留着梦的一点荣光，
四处握手，道了最后的别，
一腿翻上他那匹雀跃的枣红马，
马咬着衔铁，急着去
大家都在的地方。这时吉本开口了，
半开玩笑，可玩笑里
连它自己的诙谐都不信：
“别贪功，卡斯特！等等我们！”
卡斯特笑着松了缰。
“不会等！”他喊。众人对他这句
话直犯咕哝，看着那匹
欢腾的枣红马冒着烟冲上冲沟，
听着沿纵队扫过的
那阵热烈的欢呼。欢呼一落，
忧郁的驮骡们作了预言，
幽灵的骡子作了应答。

## XII High Noon on the Little Horn / 十二 小角河上的正午

## XII High Noon on the Little Horn /

## XII High Noon on the Little Horn /

## XII High Noon on the Little Horn /

## XII High Noon on the Little Horn / 
===Original===

Now it came to pass,

That late June morning on the Greasy Grass,

Two men went fishing, warriors of the Sioux;

And, lonesome in the silence of the two,

A youngster pictured battles on the sand.

Once more beneath the valor of his hand

The execrated troopers, blotted out,

Became a dust. Then, troubled with a doubt,

He ventured: “Uncle, will they find us here﻿—

The soldiers?” ’Twas a buzzing in the ear

Of Red Hawk where he brooded on his cast.

“The wind is coming up,” he said at last;

“The sky grows dusty.” “Then the fish won’t bits,”

Said Running Wolf. “There may be rain tonight”

Said Red Hawk, falling silent. Bravely then

The youngster wrought himself a world of men

Where nothing waited on a wind of whim,

But everything, obedient to him,

Fell justly. All the white men in the world

Were huddled there, and round about them swirled
